

######## ANGLE: benchmarks ########
angle: Benchmarks and metrics for OBJECT hallucination in multimodal LLMs / LVLMs: what each measures, image provenance (real vs synthetic/edited), question format, metrics, size, license, harness support (lmms-eval, VLMEvalKit), known criticisms/saturation, and the gap a paired synthetic+real benchmark could fill.

[1] CHAIR (Caption Hallucination Assessment with Image Relevance), Rohrbach et al. (2018; EMNLP 2018) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.02156
    what: Canonical metric for object hallucination in free-form captions on MSCOCO: CHAIRi = hallucinated object mentions / all object mentions, CHAIRs = captions containing at least one hallucinated object / all captions. Caption words are mapped to the 80 COCO categories through a fixed synonym list; ground truth is the union of instance-segmentation labels and objects named in the reference captions. There is no official LVLM split; papers usually caption ~500 COCO val2014 images with a 'describe this image in detail' prompt.
    why: It is the only widely used metric that scores open generation (the setting Kalai et al. argue is distorted by guess-rewarding evaluation), but it is closed-vocabulary (anything outside 80 classes is invisible), the synonym table both misses and excuses errors (ALOHa, NAACL 2024, https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02904: 'baseball player'/'bat' missed, 'catcher'/'umpire' wrongly excused, adjective 'orange' parsed as a noun), it can be gamed by naming fewer objects, and the POPE authors showed that changing the instruction can double CHAIR and reorder models. Open-vocabulary successors you should cite: OpenCHAIR (ACL 2024 Findings, ~5K SDXL images generated from Llama-2 captions, LLM-judged, https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.03631) and THRONE (CVPR 2024, LM-judged free-form 'Type I' hallucination that is often anti-correlated with yes/no 'Type II' scores, https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05256, code CC-BY-NC-4.0).
    code/data: https://github.com/LisaAnne/Hallucination (BSD-2-Clause; needs COCO instances + captions annotations). Not a built-in task in lmms-eval or VLMEvalKit (neither task list contains a chair task); mitigation papers reuse the OPERA/HALC scripts.

[2] POPE (Polling-based Object Probing Evaluation), Li et al. (2023; EMNLP 2023) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10355
    what: Binary probing 'Is there a {object} in the image?' on 500 COCO val2014 images with >3 ground-truth objects, 6 questions per image (3 yes / 3 no) = 3,000 questions per negative-sampling setting (random, popular, adversarial = top co-occurring absent objects), 9,000 total; metrics Accuracy/Precision/Recall/F1 (F1 is primary) plus the Yes ratio. SEEM-segmented variants extend it to A-OKVQA and GQA without manual labels.
    why: The de-facto object-hallucination benchmark and the clearest primary evidence of yes-bias: in the original Table 3, mPLUG-Owl, LLaVA and MultiModal-GPT answered 'yes' to 95-100% of questions (accuracy ~50, recall ~100), while MiniGPT-4 and InstructBLIP sat at 55-69%. It is now saturated: InternVL3 reports POPE 89.6 (2B) vs 90.8 (78B), i.e. ~1 point across a 40x size range, and the HQM audit found test-retest 0.9996 but parallel-forms reliability only 0.356 plus >10% invalid items from COCO annotation noise. POPEv2 (Aug 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04567) masks objects out of training images and shows models still answer 'yes' (training bias).
    code/data: https://github.com/RUCAIBox/POPE (MIT); HF lmms-lab/POPE (9,000 rows, 500 unique COCO images, license not stated). Harness: lmms-eval tasks pope, pope_adv, pope_pop, pope_random, pope_full; VLMEvalKit dataset 'POPE' (Y/N type).

[3] MME hallucination-relevant perception subsets (existence, count, position, color), Fu et al. (2023; arXiv Jun 2023; abs page now lists NeurIPS 2025 Datasets track (Spotlight)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.13394
    what: Four coarse perception subtasks, each 30 COCO-sampled images with two manually written yes/no instructions per image (one 'yes', one 'no'), i.e. 60 questions per subtask, 240 in total; metrics acc (per question) and acc+ (both questions of a pair correct), score = acc + acc+ with a 200-point ceiling per subtask. Whole MME is ~2,800 images / 5,600 pairs across 14 subtasks.
    why: Tiny and ceilinged: InternVL2-2B and -4B already score 200.0 on existence, so it only serves as a sanity check for small models. Its paired yes/no-per-image design is, however, a good template for consistency scoring (acc+) in a new benchmark.
    code/data: Data via the Evaluation branch of https://github.com/BradyFU/Awesome-Multimodal-Large-Language-Models; HF lmms-lab/MME (2,374 rows); license not stated. Harness: lmms-eval task mme; VLMEvalKit 'MME'.

[4] MMHal-Bench (from LLaVA-RLHF, 'Aligning Large Multimodal Models with Factually Augmented RLHF'), Sun et al. (2023; arXiv Sep 2023) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.14525
    what: 96 open-ended, adversarially written questions over OpenImages images, organised as 8 question categories (object attributes, counting, spatial relations, etc.) x 12 object topics; GPT-4 scores each answer against a human answer and a list of image contents, giving an average score (0-6) and a hallucination rate.
    why: Standard generative companion to POPE in alignment papers (LLaVA-RLHF, RLHF-V, HA-DPO), so you need it for comparability, but it is small, judge-dependent and was the least reliable benchmark in the HQM audit (test-retest 0.88, human agreement 0.75). Cheap to run: 96 judge calls per model.
    code/data: HF https://huggingface.co/datasets/Shengcao1006/MMHal-Bench (Apache-2.0; eval_gpt4.py needs an OpenAI key). No lmms-eval or VLMEvalKit task found.

[5] AMBER (An LLM-free Multi-dimensional Benchmark), Wang et al. (2023; arXiv Nov 2023 (v2 Feb 2024)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.07397
    what: 1,004 images (unannotated COCO-2014 test images + Unsplash CC0 photos) with 337 annotated objects, attributes and relations plus per-image 'hallucinatory target' lists; 15,220 queries = 1,004 generative 'describe' prompts scored LLM-free with CHAIR, Cover, Hal and Cog, and 14,216 yes/no discriminative queries (existence 4,924, attribute 7,628, relation 1,664) scored with Acc/P/R/F1; AMBER Score = 1/2 (1 - CHAIR + F1).
    why: The most used generative + discriminative pair after POPE/CHAIR and the only LLM-free generative scorer, so it fits a zero-API budget. Caveats: HQM found AMBER-d parallel-forms reliability 0.36 and >10% invalid items; ODE (same group) later paired 1,004 natural images with synthetic counterparts and found them close in CLIP space, a direct precedent for a paired design (that the 1,004 are AMBER's images is my inference from the count and shared authors).
    code/data: https://github.com/junyangwang0410/AMBER (Apache-2.0; images via Google Drive). Harness: VLMEvalKit 'AMBER' (Y/N discriminative part); no amber task in lmms-eval.

[6] HallusionBench, Guan et al. (2024; CVPR 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.14566
    what: 346 images / 1,129 human-written yes/no questions arranged as control groups: Visual Dependent (original internet image vs a human-edited version of it) and Visual Supplement (text-only vs with image); metrics qAcc (question-pair), fAcc (per figure), aAcc (all), plus yes/no-bias and consistency analyses that separate language hallucination from visual illusion.
    why: Still discriminative for current models: Qwen2.5-VL-7B 52.9, GPT-4o 55.0, Qwen2.5-VL-72B 55.2, InternVL3-78B 59.1 (mean of the three accuracies). Its edited-vs-original pairs are the closest existing 'paired manipulated image' design, but much of it is charts, maps and optical illusions rather than object existence, and it is small.
    code/data: https://github.com/tianyi-lab/HallusionBench (BSD-3-Clause); HF rayguan/HallusionBench and lmms-lab/HallusionBench (1,129 rows: 951 with image, 178 text-only). Harness: lmms-eval task hallusion_bench_image; VLMEvalKit 'HallusionBench'.

[7] VHTest (Visual Hallucinations of Multi-modal LLMs), Huang et al. (2024; ACL 2024 Findings) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14683
    what: 1,200 visual-hallucination instances in 8 modes (existence, shape, color, orientation, OCR, size, position, counting). Seeds are found in COCO, turned into text descriptions, then DALL-E 3 generates the test images; released as both an open-ended (OEQ) and a yes/no (YNQ) benchmark, and fine-tuning on it reduces hallucination without hurting other benchmarks.
    why: Early evidence that T2I-generated images elicit object hallucinations in a controllable, mode-specific way and can be reused as training data; weakness for your purpose is that no matched real counterpart exists per instance and the DALL-E 3 image redistribution terms are unclear.
    code/data: https://github.com/wenhuang2000/VHTest (Apache-2.0). No harness support found.

[8] HaloQuest, Wang et al. (Google) (2024; ECCV 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15680
    what: 7,748 VQA examples (7,140 train / 608 eval) over 3,202 real Open Images photos and 4,546 Midjourney-generated images; question types: false premise (3,002), visually challenging (3,156), insufficient context (1,590); open-ended answers graded by an LLM Auto-Eval (Gemini) that agrees with humans at r=0.99; open-source models scored below 36%.
    why: The closest published precedent for a synthetic + real design: it reports that benchmark results on generated images correlate r=0.97 with those on real images. Its false-premise questions are the visual analogue of HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal task and of the abstention argument in Kalai et al., so it is the natural bridge from the advisor's text-only list. Needs an LLM judge, but only 608 eval items.
    code/data: https://github.com/google/haloquest (code Apache-2.0, data CC-BY-4.0; images are URLs into Open Images v7 and the Midjourney Showcase, each with its own terms). No harness support found; SHROOM-Visions 2026 reused its non-synthetic images.

[9] AutoHallusion, Wu et al. (2024; EMNLP 2024 (per repository)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10900
    what: Automatic benchmark generation via three manipulations: abnormal-object insertion, paired (co-occurring) object insertion, and correlated-object removal, applied to DALL-E-synthesised scenes and to COCO val images with segmentation masks; 3,129 images with yes/no existence and spatial-relation questions; hallucination-induction success 97.7% (synthetic) / 98.7% (real); GPT-4V-Turbo 66.0% overall (68.5% synthetic vs 62.9% real), Claude 3 37.1%.
    why: The one benchmark that already reports synthetic and real splits of the same manipulation, so any new paired benchmark must compare against it; model coverage stops at 2024 models and it never tests small open VLMs, leaving an obvious extension.
    code/data: https://github.com/wuxiyang1996/AutoHallusion (BSD-3-Clause; data on Google Drive). No harness support found.

[10] ROPE (Recognition-based Object Probing Evaluation) / 'Multi-Object Hallucination in VLMs', Chen et al. (2024; NeurIPS 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06192
    what: Multi-object probing on MSCOCO-Panoptic and ADE20K images: 5 objects per image are marked with red numbered boxes and the model must name each class (50 candidates) in a fixed format; splits In-the-Wild, Homogeneous, Heterogeneous, Adversarial (AAAAB); default, student-forcing and teacher-forcing protocols; accuracy. HF copy has 10,322 rows (5,170 train / 5,152 val).
    why: Avoids yes/no acquiescence bias entirely (recognition format) and shows hallucination grows with object count and class mix: GPT-4o 71.3% in-the-wild vs 60.8% heterogeneous, LLaVA-7B 35.3% vs 17.4%. A cheap, still-discriminative alternative to POPE for 2-7B models; object co-occurrence/salience effects echo the co-occurrence priors Huang et al. describe for text LLMs.
    code/data: https://github.com/sled-group/moh; HF https://huggingface.co/datasets/sled-umich/ROPE (Apache-2.0 on HF; project page says CC BY-SA 4.0, conflicting). No harness support found.

[11] R-Bench (relationship hallucination) and Reefknot (relation hallucination) (2024 / 2025; ICML 2024 / ACL 2025 Findings) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16449
    what: R-Bench: yes/no image-level and instance-level (box or mask) relationship questions on Open Images v4 validation, with POPE-style random/popular/adversarial negatives built from relation co-occurrence; finds LVLMs ignore the image and lean on LLM common sense, and fail on long-tail relations. Reefknot (https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09429): 20k+ Visual Genome scene-graph samples in Yes/No, multiple-choice and VQA formats, perceptive vs cognitive relations, plus a Detect-then-Calibrate decoding fix (-9.75% hallucination).
    why: Both show that object-level detectors fix object existence but not relations; if your synthetic scenes are generated from a scene graph you get relation ground truth for free and can test the same models at object and relation level.
    code/data: https://github.com/mrwu-mac/R-Bench (Apache-2.0; Google Drive data); https://github.com/JackChen-seu/Reefknot (no license stated). No harness support found.

[12] Hallu-PI (perturbed inputs) and SHALE (scalable synthetic fine-grained hallucination benchmark) (2024 / 2025; ACM MM 2024 / arXiv Aug 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01355
    what: Hallu-PI: 1,260 perturbed images (noise, blur, weather, digital, concatenation, cropping, prompt misleading) over 11 object types with existence/attribute/relation labels, yes/no and captioning tasks; 12 models hallucinate under perturbation but not on the clean originals. SHALE (https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09584): 30K+ image-instruction pairs on Stable Diffusion 3.5 images filtered with VQAScore and manual checks, 12 faithfulness aspects + 6 factuality domains, 10 image/instruction perturbations, YNQ/MCQ/free-form/captioning, LLM-judge for generative, 'Resistance Rate'; LLaVA-1.5-7B falls from 78.8% clean to 37-40% under misleading instructions.
    why: SHALE is the largest fully synthetic hallucination benchmark and documents a reproducible T2I + VQAScore-filter pipeline you could replicate at small scale; both show that clean-image scores overstate robustness, which matters when you compare real photos with generated ones.
    code/data: https://github.com/NJUNLP/Hallu-PI (MIT; Google Drive); https://github.com/BeiiiY/SHALE (OneDrive; license not stated). No harness support found.

[13] PhD (A ChatGPT-Prompted Visual Hallucination Evaluation Dataset), Liu et al. (2025; CVPR 2025 (Highlight)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11116
    what: 102,564 yes/no VQA triplets over 14,648 COCO-2014 images plus 750 counter-common-sense (CCS) images generated with Doubao and DALL-E 3; five tasks (object, attribute, sentiment, position, counting); four modes: base, sec/iac (specious context), icc (incorrect context), ccs (generated counter-intuitive images); PhD Index = F1 of yes-recall and no-recall to expose answer bias. Reported: GPT-4o 0.812, Claude 3.5 Sonnet 0.746, LLaVA-OneVision 0.698, InternVL-1.5 0.561; icc and ccs are hardest because 'models favor the textual part'.
    why: Largest yes/no set with a built-in bias-aware metric and a generated-image split, so it is directly usable for a synthetic-vs-real comparison and for bridging Kalai et al.'s point that text priors beat evidence. HF copy is 17,597 rows (one row = yes+no question pair), MIT-licensed.
    code/data: https://github.com/jiazhen-code/PhD; HF https://huggingface.co/datasets/AIMClab-RUC/PhD (MIT). No harness support found.

[14] ODE (Open-Set Evaluation of Hallucinations in MLLMs), Tu, Hu, Sang (2025; CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09318
    what: Dynamic, regenerable protocol: a weighted concept graph (objects, attributes, co-occurrence) yields concept pairs at four distribution levels (standard, long-tail, random, fictional); prompts go to Stable Diffusion 1.5 / FLUX.1-dev; YOLO-World filters images (confidence >= 0.65); outputs yes/no existence and attribute queries plus 'describe' captioning. Reported set: 40 concept combinations, 808 images, 8,786 test pairs; metrics Acc/P/R/F1 and CHAIR/Cover/Hal/Cog.
    why: Key evidence for your thesis: models score higher on COCO-2014 than on equally distributed internet or generated images (data contamination), while generated and internet images behave alike and sit close in CLIP space (1,004 natural-synthetic pairs). Because SD 1.5 fits a 6 GB GPU, you can regenerate fresh contamination-free test sets locally.
    code/data: https://github.com/Iridescent-y/ODE (pipeline code; no license stated; YOLO-World submodule).

[15] HQM / HQH ('Measuring the Measurers: Quality Evaluation of Hallucination Benchmarks for LVLMs'), Yan et al. (2024; arXiv (v3)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17115
    what: A benchmark-quality framework (HQM) measuring test-retest and parallel-forms reliability, criterion validity (agreement with humans) and hallucination-type coverage, applied to POPE, AMBER-d/g, OpenCHAIR, MMHal and GAVIE; it releases HQH, 4,000 free-form VQA pairs on Visual Genome (500 per each of 8 dimensions: existence, count, color, action, spatial, comparison, environment, text), judged by GPT-4o.
    why: The primary reference for your criticism section: yes/no benchmarks show acquiescence/dissent bias and unstable rankings across parallel forms (POPE 0.356, AMBER-d 0.364), POPE/AMBER-d carry >10% invalid items from COCO annotation noise, and LLM-judged sets have lower human agreement (MMHal 0.75, OpenCHAIR 0.68). Reusing its reliability protocol to validate your own benchmark would make the report far stronger than another score table.
    code/data: https://github.com/HQHBench/HQHBench (annotations in repo, images via OneDrive; license not stated).

[16] HALLUCINOGEN (object-hallucination 'attack' prompts), Seth, Manocha, Agarwal (2025; UncertaiNLP 2025 workshop at EMNLP 2025 (arXiv Dec 2024)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.20622
    what: 90,000 image-prompt pairs = 6,000 visual-entity pairs (3,000 MS-COCO salient objects + 3,000 NIH chest X-ray latent findings) x 15 implicit prompts of four types (localization, visual context, counterfactual, identification); answers are reduced to yes/no by string matching or a GPT-4o judge; 11 LVLMs (LLaMA-3.2, DeepSeek-V2, Gemini, etc.) remain vulnerable; MED-HALLUCINOGEN extends to medical images.
    why: Explicitly argues that POPE-style single-object explicit questions are too easy and that implicit contextual prompts re-expose hallucination; a cheap way to make a saturated POPE-style set discriminative again on your paired images.
    code/data: none found (neither the arXiv page nor the ACL Anthology page lists a repository).

[17] Common-O Bench ('What's in Common? Multimodal Models Hallucinate When Reasoning Across Scenes'), Ross et al. (Meta FAIR) (2025; NeurIPS 2025 Datasets & Benchmarks) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03768
    what: 10,586 two-image multiple-choice items ('which objects appear in both?', 3-7 objects per scene) plus Common-O Complex (12,600 items, 8-16 objects, synthetic only); images are 45% new photos taken by the authors and 55% Unreal Engine 5.4 renders from Aria Digital Twin assets, 129 object types; metrics exact-set accuracy and hallucination rate (false-positive ratio).
    why: Shows a contamination-free real + synthetic design at scale and that perception-saturated models collapse when reasoning across scenes: GPT-4o 35%, Qwen2.5-VL-7B 13.3%, LLaVA-OneVision-7B 2.6%, <1% on Complex; hallucinations rise when similar objects are present, i.e. co-occurrence priors. Its hallucination-rate metric and dual-provenance images are a model for your benchmark.
    code/data: HF https://huggingface.co/datasets/facebook/Common-O (CC BY-NC 4.0).

[18] Causal-HalBench (AAAI 2026) and DO-Bench (Apr 2026): cause-attribution benchmarks on edited or generated images (2026; AAAI 2026 / arXiv Apr 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10268
    what: Causal-HalBench: 757 COCO-val originals + 1,387 FLUX-ControlNet-inpainted counterfactuals (SAM masks) = 2,144 images / 9,709 yes/no questions; causal metrics CAC, AAC and CHR (counterfactual hallucination rate); 9 LVLMs incl. Qwen2.5-VL-7B, LLaVA-NeXT-8B, GPT-4o, Gemini 1.5 Pro. DO-Bench (https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22822): 124 GPT-image-1.5-synthesised scene groups -> 1,240 samples (66 anomalous-present, 77 expected-absent categories) + 50 LVIS real images as a sanity check; interventions escalate the textual prior (levels 0-3) or concentrate visual evidence (full/cluster/crop); metrics PriorRobust and PerceptionAbility; 14 models, Gemini-3-Flash best at 88.5%. Closely related 2026 work: ReactBench (May 2026, 4,678 Visual Genome/FSC147 images edited with Qwen-Image-Edit-2511, 50,012 QA in MC/YN/short-answer, models 40-60%, https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29579) and CIPHER's OHC-25K (CVPR 2026, 25K diffusion-edited counterfactuals, https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10470).
    why: These define the 2026 state of the art for synthetic/edited-image hallucination evaluation: controlled manipulations that attribute failures to language priors vs perception. A paired real/synthetic benchmark must position against them, but DO-Bench and ReactBench data are not yet public per their arXiv pages and none of them targets sub-4B open models, leaving room for an open, small-model-focused release.
    code/data: https://github.com/zhexu-ustc/Causal-HalBench (Google Drive data; no license stated); DO-Bench 'will be publicly released'; ReactBench project page https://reactbench.github.io/ (not yet released); CIPHER project page via arXiv.

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No existing benchmark has matched triplets (real photo, T2I regeneration of the same scene description, inpainted edit of the real photo) with identical questions. ODE compares distributions, AutoHallusion pairs by manipulation type, HaloQuest and Common-O mix provenances without per-item pairing, and Causal-HalBench/POPEv2/OHC-25K edit only in one direction. A per-item paired design lets you test the provenance effect statistically (e.g., McNemar on matched yes/no items) and report it per hallucination mode; nobody reports this today.
  * Abstention is not scored anywhere in the object-hallucination literature: every benchmark here is yes/no accuracy/F1 or judge-scored, and none gives credit for 'I cannot tell' or penalises a confident wrong 'yes' more than an abstention. Bringing Kalai et al.'s abstention-aware scoring and HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal task into the visual setting (false-premise questions on your paired images, with an explicit abstain option and a calibration/Yes-ratio report) is a clear, cheap contribution.
  * Small open VLMs that fit a 6 GB GPU (roughly 1-4B parameters) are rarely in the 2025-2026 tables, which mostly report 7B+ open models and proprietary APIs. A consistent small-model comparison across POPE, AMBER-d, ROPE, PhD-ccs, ODE-style regenerated sets and your paired set would be citable on its own.
  * Benchmark reliability is almost never reported: only HQM measured test-retest and parallel-forms stability. A new benchmark that ships with those numbers, a Yes-ratio / PhD-Index bias control, and ODE-style regenerability would stand out from the dozens of 2025-2026 score-table benchmarks.
  * Detection transfer is untested: LLM-Check-style hidden-state/attention detectors now have 2026 visual analogues (VisER, EMNLP 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30480; HaloProbe, Apr 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06165), but none asks whether a detector trained on real images transfers to synthetic ones or vice versa. That is the most direct bridge from the advisor's text-only reading list to the multimodal title.
  * Contamination: POPE, CHAIR, AMBER (partly), PhD-base, Causal-HalBench and Hallucinogen all sit on COCO; ODE shows COCO scores are inflated relative to equally distributed fresh images. Fresh images (own photos as in Common-O, or regenerated synthetic sets) are the only clean comparison.
  * Span-level and generation-side evaluation are moving fast but are still object-agnostic: SHROOM-Visions 2026 / SHEEP (20k span-annotated VLM outputs in 4 languages, images reused from HaloQuest and VISaGE, CC-BY-NC, https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25662), DetailVerifyBench (1,000 images, >200-word captions, token-level spans, https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05623), VOPE (ACM MM 2026, models fail to re-check objects they themselves imagined, https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13420) and MIHBench (multi-image, https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00726). None links span-level errors back to image provenance.
  * Licensing gap: several synthetic benchmarks rely on DALL-E 3, Midjourney or GPT-image images with unclear redistribution terms; a benchmark built on SD 1.5/3.5 or FLUX.1-dev plus own photos with an explicit license would be more reusable.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Use as the core, zero-API evaluation stack on the GTX 1060: POPE (9,000 yes/no) and AMBER discriminative (14,216 yes/no) through VLMEvalKit (which supports POPE, AMBER, HallusionBench, MME), HallusionBench through lmms-eval (hallusion_bench_image) or VLMEvalKit, ROPE and PhD-ccs for discriminative headroom, and for generation CHAIR on ~500 COCO images plus AMBER's LLM-free generative metrics. Yes/no items need one output token, so a 2-3B model in 4-bit can finish the whole stack in hours (my estimate, not measured); captioning sets are the slow part.
  * Replicate, at small scale, ODE's generation pipeline (concept pairs -> SD 1.5 on the 1060, or FLUX.1-dev on a cloud GPU -> detector filter -> templated questions) and AutoHallusion's insertion/removal edits, but build matched pairs: caption a real COCO/own photo, regenerate it with T2I, and inpaint one object away. Report per-item provenance effects, Yes-ratio, PhD-Index-style bias, and an HQM-style test-retest / parallel-forms check.
  * Cite as canonical (must appear in the related-work table): CHAIR, POPE, MME existence/count, MMHal-Bench, AMBER, HallusionBench, HaloQuest, AutoHallusion, ROPE, PhD, ODE, THRONE, plus HQM for benchmark reliability and R-Bench/Reefknot for relations. Cite as 2025-2026 state of the art: Common-O, Causal-HalBench, POPEv2, DO-Bench, ReactBench, SHALE, Hallucinogen, CIPHER/OHC-25K, VOPE, SHROOM-Visions 2026, VisER, HaloProbe.
  * Bridge to the advisor's text-only list explicitly: (a) HaloQuest false-premise and PhD-icc questions are the visual version of HalluLens NonExistentRefusal; (b) POPE Yes-ratio and HQM acquiescence bias are the visual version of Kalai et al.'s 'evaluations reward guessing', so add an abstain option and report abstention-aware scores; (c) VisER/HaloProbe are the visual counterparts of LLM-Check, so a small real-vs-synthetic detector-transfer experiment is a natural second contribution.
  * Avoid as headline metrics: MME existence (ceiling 200 already reached by 2B models) and POPE-random (~90 for everything); avoid GPT-4-judged MMHal-Bench or HQH as the main result (judge cost and lower reliability), but MMHal's 96 items are cheap enough to include as a secondary number.
  * Avoid building a redistributable benchmark on DALL-E 3, Midjourney or GPT-image outputs (VHTest, HaloQuest synthetic half, PhD-ccs, DO-Bench) because of unclear image terms; prefer Stable Diffusion (check SD 3.5 community license), FLUX.1-dev (non-commercial license) or your own photos, and state the license explicitly.
  * Prefer recognition-format (ROPE-style) or paired yes/no-with-consistency (MME acc+, HallusionBench qAcc) questions over plain yes/no to blunt acquiescence bias; use Hallucinogen-style implicit prompts if the plain questions saturate on your models.

-- cautions --
  * The shared web-search budget ran out part-way through the sweep (about 12 searches completed); 2025-2026 coverage therefore relies on those searches plus the Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination list, and the Hugging Face dataset search page did not render. Benchmarks I saw only as titles and did not open: MVI-Bench (2511.14159), EgoIllusion (EMNLP 2025), LanP (2502.12359), Antidote/CP-Bench (CVPR 2025, opened abstract only), HalluSegBench (opened abstract only), DynaHall (2609.34772, abstract only), VisER and HaloProbe (abstracts only). There may be further 2026 object-hallucination benchmarks I missed.
  * Several figures come from WebFetch summaries of HTML pages and could contain transcription errors. One concrete example: the summariser first reported POPE as '3 questions per image, ~1,500 total' with approximate Yes ratios; I corrected this by extracting the ACL PDF text directly (500 images x 6 questions = 3,000 per setting; Yes ratios 95-100% for LLaVA/mPLUG-Owl/MultiModal-GPT). Re-check any number you cite against the paper.
  * Leaderboard numbers quoted for saturation (InternVL3 POPE 89.6-90.8 from the InternVL3 blog; Qwen2-VL POPE 90.67/89.33/87.27 from a third-party paper snippet; Qwen2.5-VL-7B 'HallBench 52.9' from its HF model card; InternVL2-2B/4B MME existence 200.0 from InternVL docs) were taken from search snippets or model cards, not re-derived; verify before citing. The Qwen2.5-VL-7B model card lists no POPE score.
  * Venue labels to double-check: the MME arXiv page now shows 'NeurIPS 2025 Datasets track (Spotlight)' although MME is a 2023 benchmark; AutoHallusion is 'EMNLP 2024' per its README (main vs Findings not verified); ALOHa's NAACL 2024 venue comes from an ACL Anthology listing I did not open; VOPE's 'ACM MM 2026 Dataset Track' and ReactBench/DO-Bench venues are as stated on their arXiv pages.
  * Licenses: ROPE is Apache-2.0 on Hugging Face but CC BY-SA 4.0 on its project page; Reefknot, ODE, HQH, Causal-HalBench, SHALE and MME state no license; HaloQuest images are URLs into Open Images v7 and the Midjourney Showcase with separate terms; DALL-E 3 / Doubao / GPT-image redistribution terms for VHTest, PhD-ccs, AutoHallusion and DO-Bench were not verified; the CC BY 4.0 / CC BY-NC-SA labels on DO-Bench, ReactBench and SHALE arXiv pages are paper licenses, not necessarily dataset licenses.
  * Not yet available: DO-Bench ('will be publicly released'), ReactBench ('will publicly release ... upon acceptance'); no code or data repository was found for Hallucinogen or VOPE.
  * HallusionBench counts: the GitHub README summary returned '254 questions / 69 figures', which conflicts with the paper abstract and the lmms-lab HF copy (1,129 questions, 346 images); I report the paper/HF numbers.
  * 'ODE's 1,004 natural-synthetic pairs are AMBER's images' is my inference (same count, overlapping authors), not an explicit statement in the text I fetched.
  * Harness checks were done on directory listings: lmms-eval has pope (pope, pope_adv, pope_pop, pope_random, pope_full), hallusion_bench (hallusion_bench_image) and mme, and no amber, mmhal, chair, vhtest or haloquest directories; VLMEvalKit's Y/N dataset file lists MME, HallusionBench, POPE, AMBER and VSR-zeroshot. I did not check VLMEvalKit's other dataset files for MMHal or CHAIR support.
  * Compute timing statements in the recommendations are estimates, not measurements.

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence (arXiv abstract/HTML plus repository or Hugging Face page opened) for CHAIR, POPE, MME, MMHal-Bench, AMBER, HallusionBench, VHTest, HaloQuest, AutoHallusion, ROPE, R-Bench, Reefknot, Hallu-PI, SHALE, PhD, ODE, HQM/HQH, Common-O, Causal-HalBench and the lmms-eval/VLMEvalKit task names. Medium confidence for the 2026 items (DO-Bench, ReactBench, CIPHER, SHROOM-Visions, VOPE, DetailVerifyBench): arXiv pages opened but data unreleased or not inspected. Lower confidence for third-party leaderboard numbers used to argue POPE/MME saturation and for the exact venues flagged in cautions. The POPE size and Yes-ratio figures were verified from the ACL PDF text; the CHAIR definitions from the ACL PDF; all other numbers are as summarised from primary pages.


######## ANGLE: mitigation ########
angle: Mitigation methods for object hallucination in LVLMs (training-free decoding/intervention, training-based alignment, post-hoc correction), scored for reproducibility on a GTX 1060 6GB / <=4B open VLMs / small cloud budget, and for relevance to a synthetic-vs-real-image study that bridges the advisor's text-only hallucination reading list.

[1] VCD - Visual Contrastive Decoding (Leng et al.) (2023/CVPR 2024; CVPR 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.16922
    what: Training-free: at every decoding step, contrast the logits obtained with the original image against those obtained with a diffusion-noised copy (alpha=1, beta=0.1, T=999 noise steps), penalising tokens the model would emit without visual evidence. Costs two forward passes per token (DeGF measured 2.01x latency). Shown on LLaVA-1.5, InstructBLIP and Qwen-VL; POPE/COCO for LLaVA-1.5: F1 81.33->87.16 (random), 77.57->81.33 (adversarial); the paper reports no CHAIR numbers.
    why: The canonical baseline every reviewer expects; implemented as a patched sampling function (evolve_vcd_sampling) so it ports in a day to any HF model including an NF4-quantised Qwen2-VL-2B/Qwen2.5-VL-3B. Its POPE gains are exactly what the 2025-2026 'Mirage'/'Rethinking CD' papers call artefacts, so re-running it on small models with the greedy and Yes-bias controls is itself a contribution.
    code/data: https://github.com/DAMO-NLP-SG/VCD (Apache 2.0)

[2] OPERA - Over-trust Penalty and Retrospection-Allocation (Huang et al.) (2023/CVPR 2024 Highlight; CVPR 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.17911
    what: Training-free beam-search decoding that penalises attention 'summary-token' over-trust and rolls back when it appears. Reported CHAIR (512 tokens): LLaVA-1.5 greedy 45.0/14.7 -> OPERA 44.6/12.8; InstructBLIP 58.8/23.7 -> 46.4/14.2; Shikra 55.8/15.4 -> 36.2/12.1; POPE average F1 gains are marginal (LLaVA-1.5 84.9->85.4). Independent measurements put overhead at ~7x greedy (DeGF) or 2,643 s vs 904 s for VCD (SID).
    why: Important to cite and to describe, but a poor re-implementation target: the repo is pinned to a custom transformers 4.29.2, needs beam search (memory-hungry on 6 GB) and is the slowest common baseline. Use the published numbers as context rather than reproducing it.
    code/data: https://github.com/shikiw/OPERA (MIT; InstructBLIP, MiniGPT-4, LLaVA-1.5, Shikra 7B)

[3] ICD / M3ID / VDD - other contrastive-decoding variants (2024-2025; ACL 2024 Findings (ICD); CVPR 2024 (M3ID); ACM MM 2025 (VDD)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18715
    what: ICD contrasts the normal instruction with a disturbed one (e.g. a 'confused object detector' role prefix) injected in the Q-Former; on InstructBLIP/MiniGPT-4 it reports +7.0 pts POPE accuracy / +7.9 F1 over baseline and +3.9 over VCD; no CHAIR. M3ID (Amazon, https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14003) is mutual-information decoding on LLaVA-13B (POPE accuracy +21%, 25% fewer hallucinated objects) with no official code found on the abstract page. VDD (https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05262) contrasts against the image-free text prior; code at yfzhang114/LLaVA-Align.
    why: All are two-pass contrastive schemes, so the Mirage/Rethinking-CD critique (item 11) applies to them too; ICD and VDD are cheap variants to include in a small-model comparison, while M3ID is cite-only.
    code/data: ICD: https://github.com/PostMindLab/ICD (Apache 2.0; InstructBLIP, LLaVA, MiniGPT-4, combinable with VCD). VDD: https://github.com/yfzhang114/LLaVA-Align. M3ID: none found.

[4] HALC - Adaptive Focal-Contrast Decoding + unified decoding benchmark (Chen et al.) (2024; ICML 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00425
    what: Uses an external detector (Grounding DINO or OWLv2) to crop candidate fields of view and contrasts their logits, plus a matching-based beam search. CHAIR (64-token setting) LLaVA-1.5 greedy 20.8/6.77 -> 13.8/5.5; MiniGPT-4 30.9/12.3 -> 17.8/8.1. Authors quote 2.4x greedy time (1.35x parallelised); DeGF measured 6.51x. The repo bundles a unified benchmark with Greedy, HALC, OPERA-Beam, VCD, DoLa, Woodpecker and LURE on MiniGPT-4v2, InstructBLIP, LLaVA-1.5 and mPLUG-Owl2.
    why: Even if HALC itself is too heavy for 6 GB (detector + VLM in VRAM), its benchmark code is the best template for a fair multi-method CHAIR/POPE harness and already contains DoLa-style layer contrast as a baseline.
    code/data: https://github.com/BillChan226/HALC (BSD-3-Clause)

[5] PAI - Paying More Attention to Image (Liu et al.) (2024; ECCV 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21771
    what: Training-free attention intervention: amplifies attention weights on image tokens in a layer range (alpha ~0.2) and subtracts pure-text logits (CFG-style, gamma ~1.1) to counter 'text inertia'. CHAIR on LLaVA-1.5 greedy 46.6/13.4 -> 24.8/6.9 (OPERA 44.6/14.4); MiniGPT-4 32.8/11.1 -> 26.3/8.8; Shikra 51.2/14.4 -> 37.6/10.0; POPE multi-turn F1 84.27 -> 86.83. Near-vanilla latency except for the optional text-only pass.
    why: One of the largest reported CHAIR drops for almost no compute; implemented as an attention hook that needs eager attention, which is exactly what a Pascal GPU (no FlashAttention) uses anyway. Strong candidate for a one-week port to Qwen2-VL-2B / SmolVLM, and it is not a two-pass CD method so it sidesteps the Mirage critique.
    code/data: https://github.com/LALBJ/PAI (MIT; LLaVA-1.5, MiniGPT-4)

[6] AGLA - Assembly of Global and Local Attention (An et al.) (2024/CVPR 2025; CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12718
    what: Training-free: a BLIP-ITM matching model with GradCAM masks prompt-irrelevant patches to build a 'local' view; logits from the original and augmented views are fused. LLaVA-1.5 CHAIR_S/I 51.0/15.2 -> 43.0/14.1 (VCD 51.0/14.9, OPERA 47.0/14.6); POPE adversarial F1 76.26 -> 81.36 (VCD 79.28, OPERA 80.69); 0.69 s/sample vs 0.56 (VCD) and 1.64 (OPERA).
    why: A plug-and-play method with honest overhead numbers and a CVPR 2025 stamp; the extra BLIP-ITM model makes it tight on 6 GB but trivial on a rented 24 GB card. Good second-tier candidate.
    code/data: https://github.com/Lackel/AGLA (no license stated in README; LLaVA, InstructBLIP)

[7] SID - Self-Introspective Decoding (Huo et al.) (2024/ICLR 2025; ICLR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02032
    what: Training-free: after the early decoder layers keep only the least-important vision tokens (CT2S) to deliberately amplify hallucination, then contrast against the normal logits. CHAIR LLaVA-1.5 greedy 49.6/14.4 -> 44.2/12.2 (VCD 46.8/13.2, OPERA 45.2/12.7); POPE adversarial accuracy 79.11 -> 83.24. ~30% faster than VCD (668 s vs 904 s; OPERA 2,643 s) on V100.
    why: Cheaper than VCD and generalises the 'perturbed view' idea to token pruning, but it is one of the three methods (with VCD, ICD) that both 2025-2026 critiques found to gain only through greedy-like sampling, so it is best used as a studied subject rather than a 'solution'. Code is a modified transformers package, so porting to Qwen-family models takes effort.
    code/data: https://github.com/huofushuo/SID (no license stated; LLaVA-1.5, InstructBLIP, Shikra)

[8] DeGF - Self-Correcting Decoding with Generative Feedback (Zhang et al.) (2025; ICLR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06130
    what: Training-free: generate an image from the draft response with Stable Diffusion v1.5 (3.8 s/image), then use it as an auxiliary reference for complementary or contrastive decoding. CHAIR (64 tokens) LLaVA-1.5: 18.4/6.1 vs VCD 24.4/7.9 and HALC 21.7/7.1; POPE random accuracy 89.03 vs VCD 87.00. Overhead 4.04x latency (13.89 s vs 3.44 s) and 1.21x memory (19.1 GB).
    why: Directly relevant to the 'synthetic visual data' theme: it is the one mitigation method that creates synthetic images at inference time, which raises the open question of whether synthetic references help or hurt on synthetic inputs. Not runnable on 6 GB (VLM + SD in VRAM) but fine on a rented 24 GB GPU for a small ablation.
    code/data: https://github.com/zhangce01/DeGF (MIT; LLaVA-1.5, InstructBLIP)

[9] Nullu - HalluSpace null-space projection (Yang et al.) (2024/CVPR 2025; CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13817
    what: One-time weight edit: compute hidden-feature differences between paired truthful and hallucinated descriptions (LURE's GPT-3.5-built pairs), take the SVD to get a 'HalluSpace', and orthogonalise the weights of a chosen layer range so inputs are projected into its null space; the edited weights are re-parameterised, so inference cost is zero. CHAIR LLaVA-1.5 20.40/7.08 -> 15.20/5.30 (OPERA 17.50/6.07); MiniGPT-4 32.4/12.2 -> 21.4/8.99; mPLUG-Owl2 22.9/8.62 -> 15.6/5.77; best POPE across all three.
    why: The best fit for a 6 GB inference budget: do the edit once (even on CPU / a short cloud session), quantise the edited model, and all later evaluation runs at vanilla speed. It also connects to the advisor's LLM-Check paper (hidden-state hallucination signals) and to the LLM-prior explanation of object hallucination.
    code/data: https://github.com/Ziwei-Zheng/Nullu (MIT; LLaVA-1.5 7B, MiniGPT-4 LLaMA-2-7B, mPLUG-Owl2)

[10] 2025-2026 head/attention-level training-free methods: MaskCD, SPIN, SIRA, V-ITI (2025-2026; EMNLP 2025 Findings (MaskCD); arXiv 2025 (SPIN, V-ITI); arXiv May 2026 (SIRA)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02790
    what: MaskCD masks the 'image heads' to build the contrastive sample instead of noising the image; evaluated on LLaVA-1.5-7B and Qwen-VL-Chat with CHAIR, POPE, AMBER, MME. SPIN (https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16411, code YUECHE77/SPIN) suppresses hallucination-linked attention heads, claiming up to 2.7x lower hallucination scores at 1.8x higher throughput than alternatives. SIRA (https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14621) builds the counterfactual reference inside one forward pass on Qwen2.5-VL and LLaVA-1.5 (no code URL on the abstract page). V-ITI (https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.03542) detects 'visual neglect' with head-level probes and intervenes only when needed (no code URL found).
    why: This is where the field moved after the CD critique: single-pass, head-level interventions that already run on Qwen2.5-VL. They are the 'latest' baselines to cite, and MaskCD (Apache 2.0, Qwen-VL support) is the most portable of them for a small model.
    code/data: MaskCD: https://github.com/Deng-Jingyuan/MaskCD (Apache 2.0). SPIN: https://github.com/YUECHE77/SPIN (license not checked). SIRA, V-ITI: no code URL found.

[11] Fragility evidence: 'The Mirage of Performance Gains' (Yin et al.) and 'Rethinking the Effectiveness of Contrastive Decoding' (Bendre et al.) (2025 (final v. June 2026) and July 2026; arXiv) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10020
    what: Mirage studies VCD, ICD and SID on LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B and Qwen-VL-Chat-7B and shows POPE gains come from (1) one-directional 'Yes' bias and (2) the adaptive plausibility constraint collapsing sampling to greedy; a trivial output-layer 'Yes' bias scores 89.6% vs VCD 88.6% vs greedy 87.1% on COCO-random. The July 2026 reproduction (https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25196) repeats this on LLaVA-1.5-7B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B and InternVL3-8B over POPE (COCO/A-OKVQA/GQA), CHAIR and LLaVA-Bench: the correction is unrelated to hallucination at every layer, random perturbations of equal magnitude do as well, and captioning gains come from narrowed sampling.
    why: These two papers define the control experiments any 2026 mitigation study must include (greedy baseline, Yes-bias control, random-perturbation control). They mirror the advisor's Kalai et al. argument that evaluations reward confident guessing: POPE rewards saying 'Yes'. Nobody has yet run these controls on <=3B models or on non-CD methods (PAI, Nullu, SPIN), which is a cheap, publishable gap.
    code/data: No code URL on either abstract page.

[12] HA-DPO (Zhao et al.) and OPA-DPO (Yang et al., Microsoft) (2023 and CVPR 2025 (oral); arXiv 2023; CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.09695
    what: HA-DPO (https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.16839) reframes hallucination as preference selection with GPT-4-built positive/negative pairs; MiniGPT-4 POPE random accuracy 51.13 -> 86.13, MME 932 -> 1326; supports MiniGPT-4, LLaVA-1.5, InstructBLIP. OPA-DPO shows that off-policy expert corrections are ignored under the DPO KL constraint, so it first LoRA-SFTs on the corrected responses, then runs DPO; with only 4.8k samples (from RLAIF-V data with GPT-4V revisions) LLaVA-1.5-7B reaches AMBER HalRate 11.6% vs HA-DPO 35.6%, POVID 34.3%, RLAIF-V 16.2%, and Object-Hal CHAIRs 13.0 vs HA-DPO 54.0. Default recipe: LoRA rank 256, 4x A100-80GB.
    why: OPA-DPO is the state-of-the-art small-data DPO recipe and its insight (train on-policy) is directly testable on a 2-3B model with a single 24 GB GPU and a few thousand pairs, which fits a cloud budget of a few dollars per run.
    code/data: HA-DPO: https://github.com/opendatalab/HA-DPO (Apache 2.0). OPA-DPO: https://github.com/zhyang2226/OPA-DPO (MIT; LoRA adapters on HF)

[13] Preference-data lineage: RLHF-V, RLAIF-V, POVID, CSR (2023-2025; CVPR 2024 (RLHF-V); CVPR 2025 (RLAIF-V); arXiv 2024 (POVID); NeurIPS 2024 (CSR)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17220
    what: RLHF-V (https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.00849) uses 1.4k segment-level human corrections with dense DPO to cut the base model's hallucination rate by 34.8%. RLAIF-V replaces humans with open-source AI feedback (dataset of 83,132 comparison pairs on HF), reporting 80.7% object-hallucination reduction for the 7B model and trained LLaVA-1.5-7B, OmniLMM-12B and MiniCPM-V. POVID (https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11411) manufactures dispreferred answers by GPT-4V hallucination injection and by feeding noisy images; CSR (https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14622) self-rewards candidate responses under a visual constraint and iterates (checkpoints iter1-3 and CSR-12K data on HF), claiming +7.62% across ten benchmarks on LLaVA-1.5 7B/13B.
    why: These are the datasets you can reuse instead of building: RLAIF-V pairs (CC BY-NC 4.0) and POVID/CSR data are enough for a LoRA-DPO run on a 2-3B model. Note POVID's 'noisy image' trick is the training-side twin of VCD's noised image, and it is the earliest use of corrupted/synthetic visual inputs for alignment.
    code/data: RLHF-V: https://github.com/RLHF-V/RLHF-V. RLAIF-V: https://github.com/RLHF-V/RLAIF-V (Apache 2.0 code; data CC BY-NC 4.0; HF openbmb/RLAIF-V-Dataset). POVID: https://github.com/YiyangZhou/POVID (Apache 2.0). CSR: https://github.com/YiyangZhou/CSR (no license stated)

[14] HALVA - Data-augmented Phrase-level Alignment (Sarkar et al.) (2024/ICLR 2025; ICLR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18654
    what: A DPA loss that penalises only the hallucinated phrase tokens (not the whole response) using generated hallucinated/correct response pairs; 21.5K pairs built from Visual Genome; LoRA fine-tuning of LLaVA-1.5 7B/13B on 4x A100-80GB for 1-3 hours. LLaVA-1.5-7B CHAIR_S 50.0 -> 41.4; AMBER F1 +8.7 to 83.4 (HA-DPO 78.1, EOS 75.6); 13B/384 variant reaches 87.9, matching GPT-4V's 87.4.
    why: Clean evidence that localised (phrase-level) supervision beats response-level DPO on the same base model, and the generated pair data plus LoRA checkpoints are public, so it can be evaluated (not retrained) on a small budget.
    code/data: https://github.com/pritamqu/HALVA (license not stated in README); LoRA weights on HF e.g. https://huggingface.co/pritamqu/halva13b-lora

[15] Data-side fixes: HalluciDoctor (Yu et al.) and 'Less is More' EOS supervision (Yue et al.) (CVPR 2024 and ACL 2024; CVPR 2024; ACL 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14545
    what: HalluciDoctor (https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.13614) cross-checks LLaVA-158K instruction data with BLIP2/InstructBLIP/MiniGPT-4 answers, removes hallucinated claims, and adds counterfactual samples to break object co-occurrence, reporting a 44.6% relative hallucination reduction. 'Less is More' shows over-detailed training data stops models from emitting EOS; Selective EOS supervision takes LLaVA-1.5-7B CHAIR_S/I from 50.0/15.4 to 36.8/11.3 and a data-scoring filter to 40.2/12.3, with LoRA/QLoRA training of ~0.6 h on 8x A6000 for Detail23K.
    why: Both show that hallucination is partly a training-data artefact (co-occurrence priors, length pressure), which is the mechanism synthetic images can be used to probe: generate scenes that violate COCO co-occurrence statistics and measure whether these cheaply trained variants hold up.
    code/data: HalluciDoctor: https://github.com/Yuqifan1117/HalluciDoctor (license not stated). EOS: https://github.com/yuezih/less-is-more (Apache 2.0)

[16] SENTINEL - Sentence-level early intervention (Peng et al.) and REVERSE - Generate, but Verify (Wu et al.) (ICCV 2025 and NeurIPS 2025; ICCV 2025; NeurIPS 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12455
    what: SENTINEL builds in-domain preference pairs with no human labels by sampling the model and cross-checking objects with GroundingDINO + YOLO-World consensus, then LoRA-trains (rank 128, 1 epoch, 7-12K samples); LLaVA-1.5-7B Object HalBench response-level 52.7 -> 4.3 and AMBER CHAIR 8.4 -> 2.9. Released checkpoints include Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct-SENTINEL, Qwen2-VL-7B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B and LLaVA-1.6 (Apache 2.0). REVERSE (https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13169) trains on 1.3M semi-synthetic verification samples and adds inference-time retrospective resampling; repo supports LLaVA-1.5-7B, LLaVA-More-8B and Qwen2.5-VL-3B; up to 12% better CHAIR-MSCOCO and 28-34% on HaloQuest.
    why: These are the only training-based methods with ready checkpoints at <=3B (SENTINEL Qwen2-VL-2B; REVERSE Qwen2.5-VL-3B), so a student can evaluate 'aligned vs base' on real and synthetic images without any training. SENTINEL's detector-consensus pipeline is also reproducible at small scale.
    code/data: SENTINEL: https://github.com/pspdada/SENTINEL (Apache 2.0; data on HF/ModelScope). REVERSE: https://github.com/tsunghan-wu/reverse_vlm (license not stated; dataset 'REVERSE Visual Instruct 1.3M' on HF)

[17] Counterfactual / synthetic-image preference line: HII-DPO (Yang et al.), ViPSy (Nam & Jeong), VIGIL (Xiao et al.) (2026; arXiv Feb 2026 (HII-DPO); arXiv Jun 2026 (ViPSy); ECCV 2026 (VIGIL)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10425
    what: HII-DPO removes salient objects from natural images by GroundingDINO-guided iterative masking, keeps only images on which the VLM still hallucinates the removed object in >=50% of 10 samples, and uses them for a Masked-Object-Hallucination benchmark (~800 images) and for preference data (8k Visual Genome images); LLaVA-1.5-7B CHAIR 52.7 -> 4.0, AMBER 35.5 -> 13.5, MOH 68.6 -> 18.8; also fine-tunes Qwen2-VL and Qwen2.5-VL; no code URL on the abstract page. ViPSy (https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28401, code yunpal/ViPSy) synthesises policy-aligned preference pairs from object cues across image variants (AMBER -35.7%, Object HalBench -24.5%). VIGIL (https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26387) penalises 'blind confidence' under attention masking and matches full-data SOTA with 25% of the preference data.
    why: This is the closest published work to the project's title: it shows scene-conditioned hallucination (the model names objects the scene 'should' contain) and uses edited images to both benchmark and train. The obvious gap: HII-DPO masks objects rather than photorealistically inpainting or generating them, and nobody evaluates whether gains transfer from COCO-style photos to fully synthetic (diffusion) scenes.
    code/data: HII-DPO: none found. ViPSy: https://github.com/yunpal/ViPSy (license not checked). VIGIL: none found.

[18] Post-hoc correction: Woodpecker (Yin et al.), LURE (Zhou et al.), LogicCheckGPT (Wu et al.) (2023-2024; Science China Information Sciences 2024 (Woodpecker); ICLR 2024 (LURE); ACL 2024 (LogicCheckGPT)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.16045
    what: Woodpecker is a five-stage training-free pipeline (concept extraction, question formulation, validation with GroundingDINO + BLIP-2 VQA, claim generation, correction by an OpenAI LLM); POPE accuracy +30.66 pts over MiniGPT-4 and +24.33 over mPLUG-Owl. LURE (https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.00754) trains a MiniGPT-4-13B 'revisor' on 5k hallucinated/corrected caption pairs (masking uncertain and co-occurring objects) and reports a 23% improvement over the previous best; checkpoints on HF (YiyangAiLab/LURE). LogicCheckGPT (https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11622) asks attribute<->object questions and flags objects whose answers do not close a logical loop, using GPT-3.5-turbo-0125 on mPLUG-Owl, LLaVA-1.5, MiniGPT-4 and Qwen-VL-Chat.
    why: Cite them as the post-hoc family and reuse LURE's paired data (it is what Nullu's HalluSpace is built from), but do not plan to run Woodpecker/LogicCheckGPT at scale: each needs a detector, BLIP-2 and many paid API calls per image, which conflicts with the budget. LogicCheckGPT's question loop is conceptually close to HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal task on the advisor's list.
    code/data: Woodpecker: https://github.com/BradyFU/Woodpecker (no license stated). LURE: https://github.com/YiyangZhou/LURE (no license stated). LogicCheckGPT: https://github.com/Hyperwjf/LogicCheckGPT (MIT)

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * No paper found in this sweep evaluates mitigation methods on matched real vs synthetic (diffusion-generated or inpainted) images of the same scene. The closest are SAVER (AAAI 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03177: photo vs stylized pairs, stylized images cause markedly more hallucination, 13 models benchmarked), HII-DPO (masked-object counterfactuals) and VOPE (ACM MM 2026 dataset track, https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13420: hallucination under voluntary imagination). A controlled 'does VCD/PAI/Nullu/SENTINEL help equally on synthetic scenes?' study is open.
  * The Mirage (2025) and Rethinking-CD (2026) critiques only cover contrastive decoding on 7B-8B models. Whether (a) <=3B models, (b) attention/head methods (PAI, SPIN, MaskCD) and (c) weight editing (Nullu) also gain only via Yes-bias or greedy collapse is untested. Running the Mirage controls (greedy baseline, output-layer Yes-bias, random perturbation of equal magnitude) on Qwen2-VL-2B/Qwen2.5-VL-3B/SmolVLM is cheap and clearly publishable at a workshop.
  * Synthetic images give label-noise-free negatives: RePOPE (https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15707, code YanNeu/RePOPE) found 9.3% of POPE positives are false and 13.8% ambiguous, which reorders model rankings. A generated or inpainted image where you know exactly which object was removed avoids this and lets you control co-occurrence (the HalluciDoctor / LURE / HII-DPO mechanism) and object count.
  * Bridging the advisor's text-only list is itself a contribution: Kalai et al. 'evaluations reward guessing' <-> Mirage's Yes-bias on POPE; HalluLens NonExistentRefusal <-> POPE negatives and LogicCheckGPT's closed loops; LLM-Check hidden-state/attention detection <-> Nullu's HalluSpace and V-ITI's head probes; 'Hallucination is inevitable' <-> the unavoidable language prior that VDD/M3ID/PAI try to subtract. No multimodal paper in this sweep frames object hallucination through that lens.
  * Head-to-head of training-free vs training-based mitigation at <=3B scale does not exist; the pieces are available for free (SENTINEL Qwen2-VL-2B checkpoint, REVERSE Qwen2.5-VL-3B, PAI/VCD/Nullu ports), so a small-model mitigation leaderboard on POPE/RePOPE/CHAIR/AMBER plus a synthetic split would be new.
  * DeGF is the only method that generates synthetic images inside the decoding loop; whether generative feedback helps or harms when the input itself is synthetic (style-mismatch) is unexplored and would be a natural ablation if a 24 GB cloud GPU is rented for a day.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Re-implement/evaluate these 4 in weeks 1-2, in this order: (1) VCD as a custom generate loop or LogitsProcessor on an NF4 Qwen2-VL-2B or fp16 SmolVLM-2B (1-2 days; the official code is a sampling patch); (2) PAI's attention-amplification hook plus optional text-only logit subtraction (near-zero overhead, works with eager attention on Pascal); (3) Nullu's one-time weight edit using LURE's paired data, then quantise the edited model so every later run is vanilla-speed; (4) evaluate, not train, SENTINEL's released Qwen2-VL-2B-Instruct-SENTINEL and REVERSE's Qwen2.5-VL-3B checkpoints against their bases. Optional 5th: MaskCD (Apache 2.0, Qwen-VL support).
  * Always run the Mirage/Rethinking-CD controls alongside: greedy decoding, a trivial Yes-bias output-layer modification, and a random image perturbation of the same magnitude as VCD's noise. Report POPE accuracy with precision/recall and Yes-ratio, not accuracy alone, and add RePOPE labels. This turns a replication into a finding regardless of which way the numbers go.
  * Use one evaluation protocol for everything you compare: CHAIR with a fixed max_new_tokens (papers use 64 or 512 and the LLaVA-1.5 greedy CHAIR_S swings from 20.4 to 51.0 across papers), same prompt, same 500 COCO images, plus AMBER (LLM-free) and POPE/RePOPE. Start from HALC's unified benchmark code or lmms-eval's POPE task rather than writing a harness from scratch.
  * Model choice for a GTX 1060 (6 GB, compute capability 6.1): bitsandbytes officially supports NF4/FP4 on CC 6.0+ and its Windows wheels for CUDA 11.8-12.6 include sm60, but LLM.int8() needs CC 7.5+, there is no FlashAttention-2 for Pascal, and no native bf16, so load in NF4 or fp16 with eager/SDPA attention. SmolVLM-Instruct (2B, Apache 2.0) lists 5.02 GB minimum GPU RAM; Qwen2-VL-2B and Qwen2.5-VL-3B (Qwen Research License, non-commercial) need NF4 and a reduced max_pixels (e.g. 256-640 visual tokens) to fit. LLaVA-1.5-7B in NF4 is borderline on 6 GB and should be treated as cloud-only. Benchmark tokens/s on day one before committing to a sample count.
  * Cloud cost estimate (official pricing pages, Oct 2026): RunPod community RTX 3090 $0.22/h, RTX 4090 $0.34/h, A100 80GB $1.19-1.39/h, H100 $1.99-2.69/h (secure cloud roughly 1.5-2x); Lambda A100 $1.99-2.79/h, H100 SXM $3.99-4.29/h; Colab Pro about $9.99/100 compute units (secondary sources only). Rough budget: a decoding-method sweep (4-5 methods x 2 small models x POPE 9k questions + CHAIR 500 captions + AMBER, with ~2x overhead for CD methods) is on the order of 20-60 GPU-hours on a 3090/4090, i.e. about $5-20; a LoRA-DPO run on a 2-3B model with 5-10k pairs is 1-4 GPU-hours on a 24 GB card (<$5); reproducing a published 7B recipe (OPA-DPO, HALVA, SENTINEL: 4x A100-80GB for 1-3 h) is roughly $10-20 per run on RunPod community but costs days of engineering. These hour counts are my estimates, not measured.
  * Avoid as re-implementation targets: OPERA (pinned transformers 4.29.2, beam search, ~7x overhead), HALC and AGLA on the 6 GB card (external detector/matcher in VRAM), DeGF on the 6 GB card (Stable Diffusion in VRAM, 4x latency), Woodpecker/LogicCheckGPT at scale (paid API + GroundingDINO + BLIP-2 per image), and any full-data training (RLAIF-V 83k pairs, REVERSE 1.3M samples, HALVA/OPA-DPO 4x A100 recipes). Cite them; do not run them.
  * Cite as canonical: VCD, OPERA, HALC, PAI, HA-DPO, RLHF-V/RLAIF-V, POVID, HALVA, Woodpecker, LURE, plus the two critiques and RePOPE; cite as 'latest' (2025-2026): AGLA, SID, DeGF, Nullu, OPA-DPO, SENTINEL, REVERSE, MaskCD, SPIN, SIRA, V-ITI, HII-DPO, ViPSy, VIGIL, SAVER, VOPE. For ongoing updates, the NishilBalar/Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination list (https://github.com/NishilBalar/Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination) is maintained through March 2026 and separates benchmarks, detection and mitigation.

-- cautions --
  * The per-turn web-search budget ran out at the very end, so one planned check was not done: Antidote (CVPR 2025, 'Mitigating LVLM Hallucinations in Counterfactual Presupposition and Object Perception', synthetic-data post-training) is mentioned only from a search snippet; its CVF PDF returned HTTP 403. Treat it as unverified until the student opens it.
  * Numbers quoted for the same baseline differ wildly across papers because of max-token limits, prompts and greedy vs sampling (LLaVA-1.5 greedy CHAIR_S: 45.0 in OPERA at 512 tokens, 20.8 in HALC at 64 tokens, 46.6 in PAI, 49.6 in SID, 51.0 in AGLA, 20.4 in Nullu, 50.0 in HALVA/EOS). Never compare numbers across papers; re-run baselines under one protocol.
  * Both 2025-2026 critiques conclude that VCD/ICD/SID gains on POPE and CHAIR are largely artefacts of greedy-like sampling and Yes-bias; all contrastive-decoding results in this list should be read with that in mind, and the same suspicion has not yet been tested for the attention/weight-editing methods.
  * Colab pricing ($9.99/100 CU, Pro+ $49.99) comes from secondary blogs because the official page requires sign-in; RunPod and Lambda prices were read from their official pricing pages on the fetch date and change often. GPU-hour estimates in the recommendations are my own rough estimates, not measurements.
  * GTX 1060 feasibility claims rest on the official bitsandbytes docs (NF4/FP4 on CC 6.0+, LLM.int8() needs 7.5+) and on SmolVLM's model card (5.02 GB minimum). I found no authoritative measurement of LLaVA-1.5-7B NF4 memory on a 6 GB card; the only hit was a non-authoritative calculator site, so treat 7B-on-1060 as unproven. The 'no FlashAttention-2 / no native bf16 on Pascal' statements are general hardware knowledge, not fetched here.
  * SPIN's evaluation on Qwen2.5-VL appeared only in a search snippet; its arXiv abstract page did not list models. SIRA, V-ITI and HII-DPO had no code URL on their abstract pages (code may exist elsewhere). M3ID has no official code that I could find.
  * Licenses to respect: RLAIF-V data is CC BY-NC 4.0; Qwen2.5-VL-3B is under the Qwen Research License (non-commercial, must display 'Built with Qwen' for derivatives); LLaVA-1.5 weights fall under the Llama 2 Community License; AGLA, SID, CSR, LURE, HalluciDoctor, HALVA, Woodpecker and REVERSE READMEs state no license in the portions read, so assume all-rights-reserved until clarified.
  * Many repos pin old stacks (OPERA transformers 4.29.2, SID a modified transformers package, VCD Python 3.9), so 'has code' does not mean 'runs on Qwen2.5-VL'; budget 2-4 days per ported method.
  * Dates for several 2026 items (Mirage final version June 2026, VOPE revised July 2026, SAVER revised July 2026, Rethinking-CD July/Oct 2026, SIRA May 2026, ViPSy and VIGIL June 2026, SAFE/EMNLP 2026 Sept 2026) are as displayed on arXiv at fetch time and some are preprints whose venues may change.

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence on the canonical methods (VCD, OPERA, HALC, PAI, AGLA, SID, DeGF, Nullu, HA-DPO, OPA-DPO, HALVA, EOS, SENTINEL, Woodpecker, LURE, LogicCheckGPT): numbers were read from the papers' own HTML tables or READMEs. Medium confidence on 2026 preprints (SIRA, V-ITI, HII-DPO, ViPSy, VIGIL, Rethinking-CD, SAFE): only abstract pages were opened, numeric claims are the authors' own, and venues may change. Medium confidence on pricing (official RunPod and Lambda pages opened; Colab from secondary sources). Low confidence on the GPU-hour estimates and on 7B-model feasibility on the GTX 1060; both need a day-one benchmark.


######## ANGLE: synthetic ########
angle: Synthetic and edited visual data for benchmarking or mitigating object hallucination in MLLMs: generated-image and counterfactual-edit benchmarks, synthetic training data for mitigation, the open tool stack (T2I, inpainting/removal/insertion, procedural 3D, open-vocabulary detectors for ground truth), evidence on synthetic-vs-real behaviour, validation protocols, pitfalls, and compute cost on a GTX 1060 / free tiers / cloud.

[1] HallusionBench (Guan et al.) (2023 (arXiv) / CVPR 2024; CVPR 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.14566
    what: 346 images and 1,129 yes/no questions built as control groups: each image is hand-edited so the same question is asked on original vs. edited versions, separating language hallucination from visual illusion. GPT-4V reached 31.42% question-pair accuracy; every other model was below 16%.
    why: The canonical edited-pair design and pair-accuracy / consistency metrics the student should reuse for any removal/insertion probe. Its reliance on manual editing is exactly what the newer automated pipelines (AutoHallusion, Causal-HalBench, ReactBench) try to replace, which frames the project's motivation.
    code/data: https://github.com/tianyi-lab/HallusionBench (BSD-3-Clause; data via Google Drive zip). Note the README counts (254 Q / 69 figures) are stale; cite the paper's 346/1,129.

[2] AutoHallusion (Wu et al.) (2024; EMNLP 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10900
    what: First automatic hallucination-benchmark generator: DALL-E-3 scene synthesis (or COCO val), abnormal/paired object insertion by background removal plus simple image stitching (chosen over diffusion inpainting to avoid artifacts), correlated-object removal with DALL-E-2 inpainting, and OWL-ViT to verify presence/absence. 97.7% / 98.7% hallucination-induction success on GPT-4V, Gemini Pro Vision, Claude 3 and LLaVA-1.5 (synthetic / real); 3,129 cases released.
    why: The direct template for a local insertion/removal pipeline that can be re-implemented with open models (SD/FLUX scenes, PowerPaint/LaMa removal, Grounding DINO/OWLv2 checks). Validation was a manual inspection finding 92.6% of probing Q/A correctly evaluated, and the authors concede edited images show 'perceivable hand-crafting evidences' - the artifact pitfall the student can measure.
    code/data: https://github.com/wuxiyang1996/AutoHallusion (BSD-3-Clause)

[3] VHTest (Huang, Liu, Guo, Gong) (2024; Findings of ACL 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14683
    what: Mines hallucination-prone COCO pairs (CLIP similarity >= 0.9 but DINOv2 <= 0.55), writes mode descriptions, and has DALL-E-3 generate 1,200 instances across 8 modes (existence, shape, color, orientation, OCR, size, position, counting). Images and answers were human-validated (kappa = 0.958 on 100 sampled instances). Fine-tuning LLaVA-1.5 on an 80% split (vision encoder unfrozen) reduced hallucination without hurting MME/POPE.
    why: Shows generated images serve as both benchmark and training data, and gives a cheap, reusable CLIP-vs-DINO mining trick for finding confusable object pairs. Existence mode maps directly onto object hallucination.
    code/data: https://github.com/wenhuang2000/VHTest (Apache-2.0)

[4] HaloQuest (Wang et al., Google DeepMind / Columbia) (2024; ECCV 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15680
    what: 7,748 VQA examples: 3,202 real Open Images photos and 4,546 synthetic images curated from Midjourney/Stable Diffusion galleries, with human-written questions on false premises, insufficient context and visually hard details. Open models scored below 36%. Synthetic-image and real-image results correlate at r = 0.97, but models do slightly better on the synthetic subset; Auto-Eval matches humans at r = 0.99; adding HaloQuest to training lifted BLIP2 from 11.2% to 33.9%.
    why: The strongest published evidence that generated images are a valid proxy for real ones in hallucination evaluation, and the counterpoint to 'AIGCs Confuse AI Too'. The same first author's JourneyBench (arXiv 2409.12953) adds a 'VQA with hallucination triggers' task on generated images.
    code/data: https://github.com/google/haloquest (code Apache-2.0; data CC-BY-4.0; images distributed as URLs)

[5] ODE: Open-Set Dynamic Evaluation (Tu, Hu, Sang) (2024 (arXiv) / CVPR 2025; CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09318
    what: A concept graph of objects, attributes and co-occurrence statistics drives prompts; FLUX.1-dev and SD 1.5 generate images; an open-vocabulary detector discards images whose expected entities score below 0.65 confidence. 808 filtered images yield 8,786 existence/attribute QA pairs. Eight MLLMs (incl. InternVL-2.5, Qwen2-VL) hallucinate more than on static AMBER, suggesting contamination; fine-tuning LLaVA on ODE error samples cut CHAIR by 1.6 and Hal by 7.6 and raised existence recall by 25 points.
    why: The closest existing design to a 'synthetic existence benchmark with detector-verified ground truth' and fully reproducible with open generators on a small GPU. The student can replicate ODE locally and add the missing real-photo control arm and human validation of the detector filter.
    code/data: https://github.com/Iridescent-y/ODE (no license stated; depends on YOLO-World for detection)

[6] PhD: ChatGPT-Prompted Visual Hallucination Evaluation Dataset (Liu et al.) (2024 (arXiv) / CVPR 2025; CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11116
    what: 102k VQA triplets over 14k daily images plus 750 counter-common-sense (CCS) images generated with DALL-E-3 and Doubao; tasks span object/attribute recognition, sentiment, position and counting, with specious or incorrect textual context modes. CCS descriptions were filtered manually; failed generations were re-prompted, region-inpainted, or discarded. Models drop on PhD-ccs (e.g., LLaVA-OneVision 0.698 overall vs 0.563 on CCS counting).
    why: Demonstrates that generated counter-common-sense scenes expose language-prior hallucination, a probe the student can reproduce with open generators. Its light-touch validation (manual filter plus inpainting fix-ups) is a weakness worth contrasting with VHTest's kappa-reported human check.
    code/data: https://github.com/jiazhen-code/PhD (license not checked)

[7] Causal-HalBench (AAAI 2026) with HalluSegBench as segmentation analogue (2025 (arXiv) / AAAI 2026; AAAI 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10268
    what: Formalizes co-occurrence spurious correlation with a structural causal model and builds counterfactual images by inpainting: SAM extracts the context-object mask, Gemini writes the counterfactual description, FLUX-ControlNet inpainting replaces the object. 757 COCO originals become 1,387 counterfactual images and 9,709 QA pairs; validation = manual filtering plus a CLIP-score check that the region now depicts the new object and no longer the old one. Metrics CAC/AAC/CHR; nine models including GPT-4o, Gemini 1.5 Pro and Qwen2.5-VL show high CAC.
    why: State-of-the-art counterfactual-inpainting benchmark; its CLIP-check + manual-filter protocol is the validation recipe to copy, and its CAC/AAC/CHR metrics give a causal framing beyond POPE accuracy. HalluSegBench (arXiv 2506.21546; 1,340 factual/counterfactual pairs over 281 classes, edits via GPT-4o image generation, masks via Grounded SAM, manual review; counterfactual fine-tuning cut segmentation hallucination 30%) is the same idea for pixel grounding.
    code/data: https://github.com/zhexu-ustc/Causal-HalBench (pipeline + metric code; data on Google Drive; license not stated). HalluSegBench page plan-lab.github.io/hallusegbench returned 404 at fetch time; use the arXiv HTML.

[8] GHOST: Hallucination-Inducing Image Generation (Yazdan Parast et al.) (2025 (arXiv) / ICLR 2026 (per iclr.cc poster listing); ICLR 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25178
    what: Optimizes an image embedding so the MLLM 'sees' an absent target object, then decodes natural-looking images with Stable Diffusion unCLIP. OWLv2 conservatively discards any image where the object appears, and 40 human annotators (3,000+ votes) confirmed absence in 86-89% of images. Hallucination success exceeds 28% on Qwen2.5-VL-7B, LLaVA-1.6 and GLM-4.1V-Thinking, versus about 1% for prior discovery methods; images optimized on Qwen2.5-VL transfer to GPT-4o at 66.5%.
    why: Shows synthetic images can be adversarially optimized rather than merely sampled, and models a two-stage detector + human absence check. Running it needs the MLLM resident on a 16-24 GB GPU, so it is a cloud/Kaggle experiment, not a GTX 1060 one.
    code/data: https://github.com/sudoparsa/GHOST (license not checked)

[9] ReactBench: Cause-Driven Benchmark for Multimodal Hallucination (Zhou et al.) (2026; arXiv preprint, May 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29579
    what: An MLLM writes editing instructions that Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 executes to build four tasks: Relational Erasure (remove a co-occurring object), Counterfactual Attribute, Alteration Tracing (before/after comparison) and Dense Counting; 4,678 images and 50,012 QA pairs. Two-stage human verification by five annotators accepted only about 40% of automatic edits, then removed under 5% more at the QA stage. Thirteen open models (Qwen, InternVL, LLaVA, MiMo-VL) score 32.5-66%.
    why: The newest edited-image hallucination benchmark and the best quantification of how unreliable automatic edits are (about 60% rejected), which justifies a validation study in the student's report. The data is not yet released and is promised only under a research-only license, so it is a citation, not a resource.
    code/data: none found (public release promised post-acceptance; project page reactbench.github.io returned 404 at fetch time)

[10] AIGCs Confuse AI Too: synthetic image-induced hallucination bias (Gao, Wang, Lin, Sang) (2024; arXiv 2024 (ACM DL entry doi 10.1145/3664647.3681467)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08542
    what: Re-generates POPE (500 images, 9,000 annotations) and AMBER (1,004 images, 15,200 annotations) with Stable Diffusion v1.5 while preserving semantics, then tests MiniGPT-4, LLaVA-v1/1.5, mPLUG-Owl and Qwen-VL. Synthetic images consistently induce more object hallucination (POPE-random accuracy gap up to 5.63% for LLaVA-1.5; CHAIR worse by roughly 1.7-8.5 points), with more numerous and more uniformly positioned hallucinated objects; the authors attribute it to visual-token deviation after the Q-Former/linear projector.
    why: The key prior result that synthetic images are not a neutral substitute for photos - but it used 2023-era 7-13B models and SD 1.5 only. A 2026 replication with Qwen2.5/3-VL, InternVL3 and FLUX/SD3.5, plus an edited-real-photo arm, is a clean and currently unfilled contribution.
    code/data: https://github.com/LucusFigoGao/AIGCs_Confuse_AI_Too (license not checked)

[11] SalArt-VQA: do VLMs understand salient artifacts in generated images? (Zhang et al.) (2026; NeurIPS 2026 (per arXiv listing)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12671
    what: 950 images with 3,681 human-authored multiple-choice questions covering artifact presence, semantic localization, spatial grounding and evidence-grounded identification, with matched real and clean-generated references. Across 20 VLMs the best model reaches 99.37% artifact-detection recall but answers all four artifact questions correctly on only 53.26% of images, exposing a sensitivity-calibration trade-off.
    why: Quantifies the 'generator artifact' pitfall from the model side: VLMs notice artifacts but mis-ground them, so artifacts in synthetic probes can change existence answers for reasons unrelated to the object of interest. Also a ready source of artifact-flagged images for a control condition.
    code/data: https://huggingface.co/datasets/salartvqa/SalArt-VQA (license not checked)

[12] Antidote (CVPR 2025) plus Synth-Align and CHAIR-DPO as baselines (2025; CVPR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20468
    what: Synthetic post-training for hallucination: DeepSeek-V2 curates captions and hallucination candidates, Stable Diffusion 3 generates images with the candidates as negative prompts, and a Grounding DINO 'factual assessor' checks each object's presence/absence before building preference pairs. LoRA (rank 64) preference optimization on LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B and LLaVA-Next-Mistral-7B gave +50% on the new CP-Bench (2,000 samples), +1.8-3.3 on POPE and 30-50% better CHAIR/SHR without external stronger-model supervision.
    why: The clearest recipe for turning generated images plus detector checks into mitigation data, feasible at small scale on a rented 24-48 GB GPU. Synth-Align (arXiv 2412.17417: SD3-medium, 9K pairs scored by ImageReward + ArmoRM, DPO on LLaVA-1.5-7B, POPE 87.6, MMHal 2.36 to 3.49) and CHAIR-DPO (BMVC 2025, arXiv 2508.20181, no image synthesis) are the comparison baselines.
    code/data: https://github.com/Wu0409/Antidote (SWIFT-based; license not checked). Synth-Align weights/data stated to be on Hugging Face (link not found).

[13] Can We Challenge Open-Vocabulary Object Detectors with Generated Content? (Mutze, Ilyas, Dorpelkus, Rottmann) (2025; arXiv preprint, June 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23751
    what: Two automated Stable Diffusion inpainting pipelines insert unusual objects (sampled from WordNet and ChatGPT) into street scenes, then test Grounding DINO and other open-vocabulary and classical detectors. Inpainted objects are frequently overlooked, and the detectors depend strongly on object location rather than semantics. A search snippet reports AP/AR falling to 19%/33% on synthetic objects, but those numbers were not confirmed on the abstract page.
    why: Direct evidence that detector-based ground truth weakens on edited images, which undercuts the automatic validation step used by ODE, AutoHallusion, GHOST and Antidote unless humans spot-check. A companion reference is 'Open-Vocabulary Object Detectors: Robustness Challenges under Distribution Shifts' (arXiv 2405.14874).
    code/data: none found

[14] Text-to-image generators that fit 6 GB VRAM (FLUX.1 schnell, SD 1.5, SDXL) and the FLUX editing models that do not (2024-2026; Hugging Face model cards) verified=True
    url: https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-schnell
    what: FLUX.1 schnell: 12B rectified-flow transformer, Apache-2.0, 1-4 steps; on a 6 GB card it only runs via GGUF Q3/Q4 quantization with CPU offload, and blog reports put a 512 px image at several minutes on a GTX 1060 (unverified). SD 1.5 (0.9B, 512 px, CreativeML OpenRAIL-M; the runwayml repo is deprecated and mirrored at stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-v1-5) runs comfortably on 6 GB; SDXL base (3B, OpenRAIL++-M) needs low-VRAM mode at reduced resolution. FLUX.1 Fill dev (inpainting) and FLUX.1 Kontext dev (instruction editing) are 12B models under the FLUX.1 dev non-commercial license and realistically need a 24 GB GPU.
    why: Fixes the local-vs-cloud split: SD 1.5 (and SD 1.5 inpainting) locally for bulk edits, FLUX/SD3-class quality on Kaggle or a rented GPU. ODE used FLUX.1-dev + SD 1.5, Causal-HalBench used FLUX-ControlNet inpainting, Antidote and Synth-Align used SD3, so the student can match prior work's generators.
    code/data: SD 1.5 https://huggingface.co/stable-diffusion-v1-5/stable-diffusion-v1-5 (CreativeML OpenRAIL-M); SDXL https://huggingface.co/stabilityai/stable-diffusion-xl-base-1.0 (OpenRAIL++-M); FLUX.1 Fill https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-Fill-dev and Kontext https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-Kontext-dev (FLUX.1 dev non-commercial)

[15] Inpainting, object removal and insertion tools: PowerPaint, LaMa, AnyDoor, SOC - and the ObjectDrop/Remove360 residual-trace warning (2022-2026; ECCV 2024 (PowerPaint), WACV 2022 (LaMa), CVPR 2024 (AnyDoor), CVPR 2026 (SOC)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/open-mmlab/PowerPaint
