    what: PowerPaint (MIT; v1 on SD 1.5, v2 on BrushNet) does object removal, text-guided and shape-guided insertion and outpainting in one model. LaMa (Apache-2.0; Fourier-convolution inpainting; CPU-capable) is the fast non-diffusion remover. AnyDoor (MIT; SD 2.1 + ControlNet + DINOv2) teleports a reference object into a masked location. SOC (arXiv 2510.09110, CC-BY-4.0) composites segmented objects with 3D layout augmentation and harmonization, yielding exact masks and boxes for free.
    why: These are the open substitutes for the DALL-E-2 / FLUX / GPT-4o / Qwen-Image-Edit steps in prior benchmarks. The pitfall: ObjectDrop (ECCV 2024, arXiv 2403.18818, Google; no code or data found) shows diffusion inpainting leaves shadows, reflections and occlusion effects of the removed object, and Remove360 (arXiv 2508.11431) shows foundation models can still infer removed objects even after inpainting - so 'object absent' ground truth from removal must be checked for residual cues, ideally by comparing LaMa vs PowerPaint vs FLUX Fill removals.
    code/data: PowerPaint MIT; LaMa https://github.com/advimman/lama (Apache-2.0); AnyDoor https://github.com/ali-vilab/AnyDoor (MIT); SOC https://github.com/weikaih04/Synthetic-Detection-Segmentation-Grounding-Data (CC-BY-4.0); ObjectDrop: none found

[16] Open-vocabulary detectors and segmenters for automatic ground truth: Grounding DINO, OWLv2, YOLO-World, SAM 2 (2023-2025; GitHub / Hugging Face (ECCV 2024, ECCV 2024 (OWLv2 NeurIPS 2023), CVPR 2024, ICLR 2025)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/IDEA-Research/GroundingDINO
    what: Grounding DINO (Apache-2.0; Swin-T 48.4 zero-shot COCO AP, Swin-B 56.7) is the strongest open detector; OWLv2 (Apache-2.0, about 0.2B params) is the detector used by GHOST and AutoHallusion (OWL-ViT); YOLO-World (GPL-3.0; S-X variants, 18.5-28.6 zero-shot LVIS AP, 36.6-46.7 COCO) is the fast detector ODE relies on; SAM 2.1 (Apache-2.0; 38.9M-224.4M params) provides masks, and Grounded SAM produced HalluSegBench's counterfactual masks.
    why: All run inference on 6 GB and give the automatic presence/absence labels every synthetic pipeline needs; Antidote's 'factual assessor' is just Grounding DINO. Per the Mutze et al. item, treat them as a pre-filter with a confidence threshold (ODE used 0.65) and add a human spot-check, and report detector-human agreement.
    code/data: OWLv2 https://huggingface.co/google/owlv2-base-patch16-ensemble (Apache-2.0); YOLO-World https://github.com/AILab-CVC/YOLO-World (GPL-3.0); SAM 2 https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam2 (Apache-2.0)

[17] Procedural and 3D scene generators with exact ground truth: Kubric, CLEVR, Infinigen (Indoors), BlenderProc, ProcTHOR (2017-2024; CVPR 2022 (Kubric), CVPR 2017 (CLEVR), CVPR 2023/2024 (Infinigen), NeurIPS 2022 (ProcTHOR)) verified=True
    url: https://github.com/google-research/kubric
    what: Kubric (Apache-2.0; PyBullet + Blender via Docker, no GPU needed) exports instance masks, depth and flow for multi-object scenes; CLEVR (CC BY 4.0; 100k rendered images with released Blender generation code) is the classic compositional probe; Infinigen and Infinigen Indoors (BSD-3) generate fully procedural photoreal rooms with furniture and household objects plus segmentation passes; BlenderProc (GPL-3.0) writes COCO-format annotations; ProcTHOR (Apache-2.0) procedurally generates houses.
    why: Gives perfect existence/count labels with zero inpainting artifacts and explicit control over object co-occurrence - exactly the spurious-correlation variable Causal-HalBench studies - yet it is almost unused for MLLM object-hallucination probes. CPU rendering overnight makes it the one route that needs no GPU at all; the trade-off is a larger domain gap than photo edits.
    code/data: CLEVR https://cs.stanford.edu/people/jcjohns/clevr/ (CC BY 4.0); Infinigen https://github.com/princeton-vl/infinigen (BSD-3); BlenderProc https://github.com/DLR-RM/BlenderProc (GPL-3.0); ProcTHOR https://github.com/allenai/procthor (Apache-2.0)

[18] Compute reality check: RunPod prices, Kaggle/Colab free tiers, and PyTorch dropping Pascal (GTX 1060) (2026; Official pricing/docs pages (fetched 2026-10-12)) verified=True
    url: https://www.runpod.io/pricing
    what: RunPod listed hourly rates: RTX 3090 24 GB $0.50, A40 48 GB $0.59, L4 24 GB $0.59, RTX 4090 $0.89, A100 80 GB $1.79, H100 $2.89-3.99; network storage $0.07/GB/month. Kaggle offers up to 30 GPU-hours/week on a P100 16 GB or 2x T4, 12-hour sessions and 20 GB disk (stated on the Ultralytics Kaggle docs; Kaggle's own page did not render). Google's Colab FAQ publishes no free-tier limits and says GPU types vary. PyTorch 2.8+ CUDA 12.8 wheels removed Maxwell/Pascal support, so the sm_61 GTX 1060 must stay on CUDA 12.6-or-earlier builds, and Pascal has no bfloat16.
    why: Rough budget for this angle: ~1,000 SD 1.5 edits plus detector checks run locally for free (minutes-to-hours); FLUX/SD3-quality generation of 2-3k images plus evaluating 3-4 open 7B VLMs on ~5k questions on an A40 is roughly 20-40 GPU-hours, i.e. about $12-25; one LoRA-DPO mitigation run on a 7B model is another 10-20 hours (~$10-20). Everything fits under about $50-100, or $0 if Kaggle's 30 h/week suffices.
    code/data: Kaggle quota: https://docs.ultralytics.com/integrations/kaggle/ ; Colab FAQ: https://research.google.com/colaboratory/faq.html ; PyTorch Pascal removal: https://dev-discuss.pytorch.org/t/cuda-toolkit-version-and-architecture-support-update-maxwell-and-pascal-architecture-support-removed-in-cuda-12-8-and-12-9-builds/3128

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * Synthetic-vs-real bias is unresolved and stale: 'AIGCs Confuse AI Too' (2024, SD 1.5, LLaVA-1.5-era models) found MORE hallucination on synthetic images while HaloQuest found models do slightly BETTER on synthetic ones. No 2025-2026 paper I found re-tests this on Qwen2.5/3-VL, InternVL3 or LLaVA-OneVision with modern generators (FLUX, SD3.5). A controlled three-arm replication (real photo / generated re-render of the same caption / edited real photo with one object removed or inserted) on the same POPE-style questions is a clean, feasible, publishable contribution.
  * No benchmark separates 'absence by editing' from 'absence by generation': AutoHallusion, Causal-HalBench, HalluSegBench and ReactBench all remove or swap objects with inpainting, while ODE/VHTest/PhD generate whole scenes. Nobody reports whether the same model hallucinates the removed object at different rates under LaMa vs PowerPaint vs FLUX Fill removal, i.e. whether residual traces (ObjectDrop, Remove360) drive the 'hallucination'. A residual-trace ablation is small and new.
  * Detector-as-ground-truth is assumed, not measured, on edited images: ODE (0.65 threshold), AutoHallusion (OWL-ViT), GHOST (OWLv2), Antidote (Grounding DINO) all trust an open-vocabulary detector, while Mutze et al. 2025 show these detectors overlook inpainted objects. A detector-vs-human agreement study on a few hundred edited images would be a reusable methodological result and strengthens any benchmark the student builds.
  * ReactBench quantifies that only ~40% of automatic edits survive human review, but its data is unreleased and research-only. An open, small, fully open-model (SD 1.5 / PowerPaint / Grounding DINO) counterfactual set with reported acceptance rates would be a useful artifact in itself.
  * Procedural 3D renders (Kubric, Infinigen Indoors) give exact labels and direct control over object co-occurrence statistics, the causal variable in Causal-HalBench, but are essentially unused for MLLM object-hallucination probes. A CPU-rendered 'controlled co-occurrence' existence/count probe is novel-ish, costs no GPU, and pairs naturally with a photo-edit arm to measure the sim-to-real gap in hallucination rates.
  * Bridge to the advisor's text-only reading list: nobody has checked whether hidden-state/attention hallucination detectors in the LLM-Check style transfer across image domains (real vs synthetic vs edited), nor analyzed absent-object answers through Kalai et al.'s abstention-vs-confident-guessing lens (HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal task has no visual analogue). Scoring 'I cannot see X / X is not present' as a calibrated abstention on counterfactual pairs is a cheap, distinctive framing.
  * Artifact confounds are now measurable: SalArt-VQA (NeurIPS 2026) shows VLMs detect artifacts but mis-ground them. No hallucination benchmark yet reports how artifact presence (e.g., SalArt-style flags or an artifact detector score) correlates with existence errors; adding that covariate to a synthetic probe is a small but distinctive analysis.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Core experiment to replicate and update: 'AIGCs Confuse AI Too' (arXiv 2403.08542). Re-create a POPE/AMBER subset in three arms (original COCO photo; FLUX.1-schnell or SD 1.5 re-render of the same caption; PowerPaint/LaMa edit of the real photo) and evaluate 3-4 current open models (Qwen2.5-VL-3B/7B, InternVL3, LLaVA-OneVision) plus a small paid sample of GPT/Gemini. This is tangible, directly comparable to prior numbers, and cheap.
  * Reuse, do not rebuild, the validation protocols: Causal-HalBench's manual filter + CLIP-score region check for edits, ODE's detector-confidence filter (>= 0.65) for generated images, and GHOST's OWLv2-then-human absence check. Report detector-human agreement explicitly; cite Mutze et al. (arXiv 2506.23751) as the reason.
  * Metrics to adopt: HallusionBench pair-accuracy/consistency for edited pairs, Causal-HalBench CAC/AAC/CHR for co-occurrence effects, CHAIR for captions, POPE accuracy/precision/recall and the yes-ratio for existence. Add an abstention/calibration score (Kalai et al. framing) on absent-object questions.
  * Local tool stack for the GTX 1060: SD 1.5 + SD 1.5 inpainting or PowerPaint v1 (MIT), LaMa (Apache-2.0, even CPU) for removal, Grounding DINO Swin-T or OWLv2 (Apache-2.0) and SAM 2 small for masks/labels. Install PyTorch CUDA 12.6 wheels (not cu128) and use fp16/fp32; Pascal has no bf16 and PyTorch 2.8+ cu128 dropped it.
  * Cloud/free-tier plan: use Kaggle's 30 GPU-h/week (P100 16 GB or 2x T4) for 7B VLM inference in 4/8-bit and for FLUX/SD3-quality generation; rent a RunPod A40 48 GB (~$0.59/h) or RTX 3090 (~$0.50/h) for anything bigger. Budget roughly $12-25 for generation + evaluation and $10-20 for one LoRA-DPO mitigation run; avoid H100s. FLUX.1 Fill/Kontext dev need 24 GB-class GPUs and a non-commercial license.
  * Mitigation experiment (upside for a paper): replicate Antidote's pipeline at small scale (SD3-medium or FLUX images + Grounding DINO factual assessor + LoRA preference optimization) on a 3B model, or simply fine-tune on ODE/VHTest-style error samples as those papers did, and compare against CHAIR-DPO as a no-synthesis baseline. Keep this as a stretch goal after the benchmark arm is done.
  * Cite as canonical: HallusionBench (CVPR 2024), POPE/CHAIR (via the surveys), AutoHallusion (EMNLP 2024), VHTest (ACL 2024), HaloQuest (ECCV 2024), ODE (CVPR 2025), PhD (CVPR 2025), Antidote (CVPR 2025), Causal-HalBench (AAAI 2026), GHOST (ICLR 2026), ReactBench (2026, preprint). Use NishilBalar/Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination (updated March 2026) and showlab/Awesome-MLLM-Hallucination to track new entries.
  * Avoid: building the probe set with proprietary generators/editors (DALL-E, GPT-4o image, Gemini, Doubao, Qwen-Image-Edit) as prior work did - costs and non-reproducibility; relying on ReactBench or ObjectDrop data (unreleased); and trusting detector labels on edited images without a human sample.
  * Consider the no-GPU control arm: a small Kubric or Infinigen Indoors render set with controlled co-occurrence (e.g., kitchen without a stove, office without a chair) rendered on CPU overnight; it gives perfect labels and a story the advisor's causal/co-occurrence framing supports.

-- cautions --
  * ReactBench (arXiv 2605.29579) has no released data; the preprint promises a research-only license post-acceptance and its project page returned 404 on 2026-10-12. Cite it, do not plan on it.
  * ObjectDrop (ECCV 2024) has no code, weights or dataset that I could find; the project page objectdrop.github.io rendered only a title. Treat it as a citation for the shadow/reflection pitfall only.
  * HalluSegBench's project page (plan-lab.github.io/hallusegbench) returned 404 at fetch time; details come from the arXiv HTML. Causal-HalBench, ODE, PhD, GHOST, Antidote and the AIGCs repo state no license or I did not check one - verify before redistributing derived data.
  * The '19% AP / 33% AR' degradation figure for Grounding DINO on inpainted objects comes from a search snippet of arXiv 2506.23751 and was not confirmed on the abstract page; the qualitative finding (detectors overlook inpainted objects, depend on location) is confirmed.
  * GTX 1060 speed claims (e.g., about 9 minutes per 512 px FLUX image; SD 1.5 in tens of seconds) are from blogs, not measured; benchmark locally before planning volumes.
  * Direction of the synthetic-image effect is generator- and probe-dependent: HaloQuest saw slightly higher accuracy on synthetic images; AIGCs Confuse AI Too saw more hallucination. Do not assume either in the hypothesis.
  * Prompt-derived labels are unreliable: text-to-image models often omit or duplicate prompted objects, so 'the prompt said there is a dog' is not ground truth; ODE discards images below 0.65 detector confidence and GHOST needed OWLv2 plus humans. Any benchmark the student builds needs the same two-stage check.
  * Edited images carry traces: AutoHallusion's authors admit 'perceivable hand-crafting evidences', ObjectDrop documents leftover shadows/reflections, and Remove360 shows foundation models can recover removed objects after inpainting. Report an artifact/residual check (e.g., a second detector on the removed region, or SalArt-style human flags).
  * Licenses: SD 1.5 (CreativeML OpenRAIL-M) and SDXL (OpenRAIL++-M) carry use restrictions; FLUX.1 Fill/Kontext dev are non-commercial; YOLO-World and BlenderProc are GPL-3.0; HaloQuest and SalArt images are distributed as URLs or under terms that must be checked before re-publication.
  * Kaggle's quota (30 h/week, P100 or 2x T4, 12-h sessions) was verified on the Ultralytics docs page, not Kaggle's own page, which did not render; Google publishes no Colab free-tier limits. RunPod prices are the secure-cloud listing on 2026-10-12 and change frequently.
  * HallusionBench's GitHub README reports 254 questions / 69 figures, which conflicts with the paper's 346 images / 1,129 questions; cite the paper.
  * Venue attributions I could not open on the venue site itself: GHOST = ICLR 2026 (iclr.cc poster URL in search results), SalArt-VQA = NeurIPS 2026 and VIGIL = ECCV 2026 (arXiv listings), AutoHallusion = EMNLP 2024 main (UMD publication page). AIGCs Confuse AI Too has an ACM DL entry (likely ACM MM 2024) that I did not open.

-- confidence_notes -- All 18 items were opened at a primary source (arXiv abstract or HTML, GitHub README, Hugging Face model card, official pricing/docs page) on 2026-10-12, so names, ids, sizes, tools used and licenses stated above come from those pages. Where a detail came only from a search snippet or a secondary blog (the Grounding DINO AP/AR numbers, GTX 1060 per-image times, Kaggle quota via Ultralytics docs, ReactBench and ObjectDrop code status, several venue attributions) it is flagged in the cautions. Search coverage: roughly 20 extended/standard web searches plus 50+ page fetches across arXiv, GitHub, Hugging Face, CVF/ACL/AAAI pages and pricing sites; 2026 preprints (ReactBench, SalArt-VQA, ViPSy, VIGIL, CFHR, VOPE) were checked and only the ones relevant to synthetic/edited images were kept. Cost estimates are order-of-magnitude, derived from listed hourly prices and typical throughput assumptions rather than measured runs.


######## ANGLE: detection_transfer ########
angle: Bridging the advisor's text-only hallucination reading list (LLM-Check internals-based detection; Kalai et al. "benchmarks reward guessing") to multimodal object hallucination in LVLMs. Part A: detection from hidden states, attention over image tokens, logit/entropy and semantic-entropy signals. Part B: abstention / IDK / refusal options, calibration on object presence, selective prediction, and whether an image analog of HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal exists. Plus low-compute study designs for a GTX 1060 and the prior work that would make them non-novel.

[1] Interpreting and Editing Vision-Language Representations to Mitigate Hallucinations (Jiang, Kachinthaya, Petryk, Gandelsman) (2025; ICLR 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02762
    what: Applies the logit lens to image-token representations inside LLaVA-style VLMs and finds real objects decode with higher internal confidence than hallucinated ones; this gives a training-free detector and spatial localizer. A linear 'knowledge erasure' edit (orthogonalizing image features against hallucinated-object features) reduces hallucinations by up to 25.7% on COCO2014 without hurting performance.
    why: The closest existing analog to LLM-Check's 'read the internals' philosophy in the multimodal setting, and the standard logit-based baseline any new internal-signal detector must beat. Cheap: one forward pass, no training, works on small models.
    code/data: Project page https://anishk23733.github.io/vl-interp/ (license not checked)

[2] Devils in Middle Layers of LVLMs: Interpreting, Detecting and Mitigating Object Hallucinations via Attention Lens (Z. Jiang et al.) (2024/2025; arXiv (v1 Nov 2024, v2 Apr 2025)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16724
    what: Shows middle layers do 'visual information enrichment' then 'semantic refinement'; during enrichment, real objects receive consistently higher attention than hallucinated ones, which serves as a per-token hallucination indicator. Also finds hallucinated tokens come from heads attending to inconsistent objects and proposes a training-free head-integration fix.
    why: This is the attention-map counterpart of LLM-Check's attention-kernel analysis. Two later attention-signal detectors extend it and are the baselines a new attention-statistic method must beat: PAS (Nov 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11502; training-free score from attention over previously generated tokens, built on the finding that hallucinating LVLMs 'effectively ignore the image') and CADMP (Sept 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02028; drift between consecutive layers' cross-modal attention maps plus mask-based verification).
    code/data: https://github.com/ZhangqiJiang07/middle_layers_indicating_hallucinations (license not checked); PAS and CADMP: no code found on abs pages

[3] Same Attention, Different Truths: Put Logit-Lens over Visual Attention to Detect and Mitigate LVLM Object Hallucination (Wang et al.) (2026; CVPR 2026 (Highlight); arXiv Aug 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07302
    what: Decodes the high-attention image regions with the logit lens and finds that real and hallucinated objects both receive strong visual attention, but only real-object regions decode to the right token. Separates two mechanisms (visual uncertainty from confusable regions vs. contextual priors from co-occurrence) and proposes training-free HARM (masking) and VEED (visual-evidence-enhanced decoding).
    why: Refutes the naive 'low image attention = hallucination' hypothesis, so a detector that only sums attention mass to image tokens is already outdated. Its decodability-of-attended-region signal is a single-forward-pass statistic a student can replicate on a 2B model.
    code/data: "Code will be available" per abstract; no repo found

[4] UniProbe: A Learnable Token-Level Hallucination Detector for Large VLMs using Multi-Structural Internal Representations (Samuel et al., NVIDIA) (2026; arXiv Aug 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10835
    what: A lightweight learned detector that reads layer hidden states and attention from a single forward pass, builds a directed graph over image patches, query tokens and response tokens, and processes it with alternating GNN / ViT / GRU blocks. A streaming variant cuts object hallucinations by up to 55% during decoding at 1.06x latency.
    why: The current frontier for learned probes over LVLM internals; it shows that structure-aware detectors are where the field is, so a plain probe on hidden states is no longer a contribution. Also a reason to position a training-free, spectral (LLM-Check-style) signal as the lightweight alternative rather than compete on learned detectors.
    code/data: Project page https://research.nvidia.com/labs/par/uniprobe/ (code/license not confirmed)

[5] HALP: Detecting Hallucinations in Vision-Language Models without Generating a Single Token (Kogilathota et al.) (2026; EACL 2026; arXiv Mar 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05465
    what: Trains lightweight probes on three internal signal families (visual-only features, vision-token states inside the decoder, query-token states) to predict hallucination risk before generation. Across eight VLMs (Llama-3.2-Vision, Gemma-3, Phi-4-VL, Qwen2.5-VL, Molmo, etc.) it reaches AUROC up to 0.93; late query-token states are most informative.
    why: This is the obvious 'first bridge' from LLM-Check (linear probes on LVLM hidden states) and it has already been done at scale, so it is prior work that makes a plain probing study non-novel. EnsemHalDet (Apr 2026, https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02784) goes further by ensembling separate hidden-state and attention-output detectors. The student must differentiate, e.g. training-free eigen-statistics, modality-block decomposition, or linking the signal to abstention.
    code/data: No code link on abs page (HALP or EnsemHalDet)

[6] GLSim: Detecting Object Hallucinations in LVLMs via Global-Local Similarity (Park & Li) (2025; NeurIPS 2025) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19972
    what: Training-free detector that combines global image-text embedding similarity (is the object semantically related to the whole scene) with local, region-grounded similarity (is it visually grounded somewhere). The paper also benchmarks existing object-hallucination detection methods and reports GLSim beats them by a large margin.
    why: A simple, strong, training-free baseline, and its comparison table is the reference list of detectors and metrics (AUROC/PR-AUC on CHAIR/POPE-style object sets) that a new method must be evaluated against. Cheap to run on a small model.
    code/data: No repo found on abs page or NeurIPS listing

[7] See What You Are Told: Visual Attention Sink in Large Multimodal Models (Kang, Kim, Kim, Hwang) (2025; arXiv Mar 2025 (reported as ICLR 2025; venue not shown on abs page)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03321
    what: LMMs allocate high attention to specific 'sink' image tokens regardless of relevance, driven by massive activations in a few hidden-state dimensions; removing the sinks does not hurt performance. Visual Attention Redistribution (VAR) reallocates that attention within image-centric heads, training-free, and improves hallucination benchmarks.
    why: Any eigen/attention-based detector must handle sinks explicitly or the spectrum will be dominated by them. Companion results: EVAS (EMNLP 2025, https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.174/) finds dense sinks in shallow layers and broadcasts the densest heads; EAZY (ICCV 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07772) shows ~1.5% of high-attention image tokens drive object hallucination and that zeroing them yields a 15% gain in unsupervised detection.
    code/data: No code links found on the abs/ACL pages for these three

[8] OPERA: Over-Trust Penalty and Retrospection-Allocation (Huang et al.), with HALC (2024; CVPR 2024 (OPERA); ICML 2024 (HALC)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.17911
    what: OPERA finds most hallucinations are tied to 'knowledge aggregation' patterns in self-attention (over-trust on a few summary tokens, neglecting image tokens) and adds a logit penalty during beam search plus a rollback strategy; training-free. HALC (https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00425) adds adaptive focal-contrast decoding with visual grounding and a specialized beam search.
    why: OPERA is the canonical attention-pattern-to-hallucination link that LLM-Check's attention-kernel idea maps onto most directly, and OPERA/HALC are the standard decoding baselines cited in every mitigation paper. Both require full generation (slow on a GTX 1060), so cite and reuse as references rather than as the student's core experiment.
    code/data: https://github.com/shikiw/OPERA and https://github.com/BillChan226/HALC (licenses not checked)

[9] VL-Uncertainty (Zhang, Zhang, Zheng), with Visual Semantic Entropy and VAUQ (2024-2026; arXiv Nov 2024; VSE: ECCV 2026; VAUQ: ACL 2026 Findings) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11919
    what: VL-Uncertainty estimates semantic entropy over responses to semantically equivalent visual and textual perturbations (10 LVLMs, 4 benchmarks). Visual Semantic Entropy (https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31407) perturbs only the image to isolate visual ambiguity from prompt sensitivity; VAUQ (https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21054) adds an Image-Information Score (uncertainty reduction attributable to the image) with core-region masking, training-free.
    why: This is the multimodal semantic-entropy line and the compute-cheap black-box arm (no attention dumps) to compare against white-box signals, mirroring LLM-Check's white-box vs. sampling comparison. VAUQ's self-evaluation score is also a ready-made Part B confidence signal for abstention thresholds.
    code/data: https://github.com/Ruiyang-061X/VL-Uncertainty (official; supports LLaVA, LLaVA-NeXT, InternVL 1B-26B, Qwen2-VL 2B-72B; no license file visible; tested on H100/A100/A6000). No code found for VSE or VAUQ

[10] Analyzing and Mitigating Object Hallucination: A Training Bias Perspective (POPEv2 / Obliviate; Li, Zhou, Zhao, Fang, Wen), with AutoHallusion (2025; arXiv Aug 2025; AutoHallusion: EMNLP 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04567
    what: POPEv2 builds counterfactual images from LVLM training data with objects masked; models still answer 'yes', and probing locates the bias in the LM head. Obliviate unlearns it by updating ~2% of parameters, tested from 2B to 72B models. AutoHallusion (https://github.com/wuxiyang1996/AutoHallusion) automates benchmark creation via abnormal insertion, paired insertion and correlated removal, yielding 3,129 yes/no existence/spatial questions over synthetic and COCO images.
    why: Directly serves the title's 'synthetic and real visual data' and Part B's guessing bias: the yes-bias on masked objects is the image version of 'confident guessing'. Both are prior art for counterfactual-image probes, so a new synthetic-edit benchmark must add something (e.g. detector robustness real-to-synthetic, or refusal scoring).
    code/data: POPEv2/Obliviate: 'will be released' per abstract, no link. AutoHallusion: BSD-3-Clause, but pipeline depends on GPT-4V/DALL-E API calls

[11] Reliable Visual Question Answering: Abstain Rather Than Answer Incorrectly (Whitehead et al.) (2022; ECCV 2022) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.13631
    what: Formalizes selective prediction for VQA: a model may abstain, and quality is judged by risk-coverage curves and the proposed Effective Reliability metric, which penalizes wrong answers more than abstentions. Multimodal selection functions improve coverage 2.3x at low risk; SOTA VQA models answer under 7.5% of questions at 1% error if they rely on softmax confidence alone.
    why: The canonical pre-LLM formalization of exactly Kalai et al.'s argument, in vision. Effective Reliability (with its penalty parameter) is the scoring rule to adopt when re-scoring POPE/AMBER-style object questions with an IDK option.
    code/data: https://github.com/facebookresearch/reliable_vqa (license not checked)

[12] Unsolvable Problem Detection (MM-UPD Bench; Miyai et al.) (2025; ACL 2025 main (arXiv v1 Mar 2024)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20331
    what: Defines UPD with three settings: Absent Answer Detection, Incompatible Answer Set Detection and Incompatible Visual Question Detection, 2,095 questions over 18 ability dimensions. LMMs that score well on standard MCQ benchmarks fail badly at withholding answers; chain-of-thought and self-reflection help only when the bottleneck is the LLM side.
    why: The established multimodal abstention benchmark and the nearest published analog to HalluLens-style 'refuse when the premise does not hold' (IVQD), but it is MCQ-level, not object-level. Useful as an out-of-distribution check for any abstention method the student builds.
    code/data: https://github.com/AtsuMiyai/UPD ; dataset https://huggingface.co/datasets/MM-UPD/MM-UPD (licenses not checked)

[13] Knowing When Not to Answer: Evaluating Abstention in Multimodal Reasoning Systems (MM-AQA; Madhusudhan, Yadav, Lacoste) (2026; arXiv Apr 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14799
    what: Builds 2,079 unanswerable instances from answerable ones along two axes (visual-modality dependency, evidence sufficiency), explicitly including questions about absent objects and contradictory evidence, and scores abstention with confidence baselines, accuracy-abstention trade-offs and calibration. Finds frontier VLMs 'rarely abstain' under standard prompting; multi-agent setups abstain more at an accuracy cost; concludes abstention-aware training is needed.
    why: The closest image analog found to HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal (absent-object questions, abstention rewarded). It already owns the headline 'VLMs rarely abstain', so a student study needs a sharper angle: object-level, language-prior-driven false premises built from POPE adversarial negatives, a false-acceptance-rate metric, or linkage to internal signals.
    code/data: No code/data link on abs page

[14] HaloQuest: A Visual Hallucination Dataset for Advancing Multimodal Reasoning (Wang et al., Google), with VQAv2-IDK and TUBench (2024; ECCV 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15680
    what: 7.7K VQA examples with false-premise, insufficient-context and visually challenging questions over synthetic and real images; open-source VLMs score under 36%; an Auto-Eval correlates r=0.99 with humans, and synthetic-image results correlate r=0.97 with real-image results. VQAv2-IDK (https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09717) is a human-marked unanswerable subset of VQAv2 with IDK-Instructions; TUBench (https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04107) crafts unanswerable questions with ten strategies across four visual domains (28 models; best ~69%).
    why: Prior art for 'ask about something not in the image' and for synthetic imagery; the r=0.97 result is the citation that justifies the project's synthetic-data component. These sets define what 'refusal on false premise' already covers so the student can position an object-existence-specific refusal set as a gap.
    code/data: https://github.com/google/haloquest ; TUBench https://github.com/NLPCode/TUBench ; VQAv2-IDK: no repo on abs page (licenses not checked)

[15] From Knowing to Abstaining: Bridging the Representation-Action Gap in Vision-Language Models (He & Chen) (2026; arXiv Sept 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32653
    what: Shows VLM internal representations already separate answerable from unanswerable visual questions, yet the models do not convert that into refusals. Introduces VAD-R (answerability diagnosis with rationales, shortcut cues removed) and Rep2Act, which aligns latent answerability with abstention actions, raising Qwen2.5-VL-3B from 56.7% to 86.3% and reaching 53.3% F1 on TUBench out of distribution.
    why: This is literally the Part A to Part B bridge: internals know, outputs guess. It makes a generic 'probe knows but model guesses' finding non-novel, but leaves object-level false premises and training-free (LLM-Check-style) signals open, and it works at the 3B scale the student can afford.
    code/data: No code link on abs page

[16] Object-Level Verbalized Confidence Calibration via Semantic Perturbation (Zhao et al.), with Seeing is Believing, but How Much? (Xuan et al.) (2025; arXiv Apr 2025; Xuan et al.: EMNLP 2025 main (arXiv May 2025)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14848
    what: CSP creates graded-uncertainty training data by Gaussian-perturbing object regions and then applies SFT plus preference optimization so verbalized confidence tracks object-level correctness. Xuan et al. (https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20236) evaluate verbalized calibration of VLMs across three model families and four task domains, find notable miscalibration, and propose Visual Confidence-Aware Prompting.
    why: Establishes that object-level overconfidence is known and that calibration on POPE-style object questions has been studied, so 'VLMs are miscalibrated' is not a finding. What remains cheap and less covered: logit-based (P(yes)) calibration of small open LVLMs across POPE's random/popular/adversarial splits, tied to abstention thresholds and Kalai-style scoring.
    code/data: No code links on either abs page

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * LLM-Check has NOT been applied to LVLMs as far as this sweep found: explicit searches for 'LLM-Check' with LVLM/multimodal/vision terms returned nothing, and the official repo (https://github.com/GaurangSriramanan/LLM_Check_Hallucination_Detection) covers only Llama-2-7B-Chat on FAVA/SelfCheck/RAGTruth. Its nearest text-only successor, LapEigvals (EMNLP 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17598, code https://github.com/graphml-lab-pwr/lapeigvals), is also text-only. Spectral ideas reach VLMs only as mitigation (Spectral Query-Key steering, COLM/EMNLP 2026 workshop, https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20419, ~4% CHAIR_s reduction), not detection. Opening: training-free eigen-statistics of attention-kernel and hidden-state Gram matrices, decomposed into image-image, image-text and text-text blocks, as an object-hallucination detector, with attention sinks removed first.
  * No exact image analog of HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal exists. HalluLens (https://github.com/facebookresearch/HalluLens, CC-BY-NC) uses fabricated entity names and a 'false acceptance rate' metric and has no image component. Closest partial analogs: MM-AQA (absent-object unanswerables, 2026), HaloQuest false premises, VQAv2-IDK, TUBench, MM-UPD IVQD, POPEv2 masked objects, AutoHallusion removals. None combine object-level, language-prior-driven false premises (POPE adversarial co-occurrence negatives) with open-ended prompts, a false-acceptance-rate metric and a Kalai-style confidence-target scoring rule. That combination is a credible, cheap benchmark contribution.
  * Kalai et al.'s remedy (change benchmark scoring to stop rewarding guesses; the abs page has no multimodal mention) has not, in this sweep, been applied to existing LVLM hallucination leaderboards. Re-scoring POPE/AMBER/HallusionBench-style yes/no results under Effective Reliability and explicit confidence targets, and showing how model rankings change, is a low-compute analysis nobody appears to have published for multimodal.
  * Detector robustness across real vs. synthetic/counterfactual images is not covered: HaloQuest shows model accuracy correlates r=0.97 across synthetic and real, but no paper found tests whether internal-signal detectors (probes, logit lens, attention statistics) trained on COCO transfer to masked/edited images (POPEv2, AutoHallusion). This ties the title's 'synthetic and real' clause to Part A.
  * Small open models (1B-3B: Qwen2-VL-2B, Qwen3-VL-2B, InternVL 1B/2B) are underrepresented in the detection papers (HALP, UniProbe, GLSim mostly use 7B+), while they are the only class a GTX 1060 can run. A systematic small-model study of which internal signals survive at that scale is itself useful.
  * Text-only abstention benchmarks (AbstentionBench, Meta 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09038; explicitly no image component) show reasoning fine-tuning degrades abstention by 24%; the multimodal counterpart question (do 'thinking' VLMs abstain less on absent-object questions?) is open but may need API models.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Primary study (Part A, novel and feasible): 'LLM-Check for LVLMs'. Port the three LLM-Check signal families (hidden-state eigen-scores, attention-kernel eigen-scores, logit entropy) to one small open LVLM and compute them separately over the image-token block, the text block and the cross-modal block, after removing visual attention sinks. Evaluate object-hallucination detection on POPE (MIT, https://github.com/RUCAIBox/POPE, 3 splits, COCO val2014, 'Is there a {object} in the image?') and AMBER discriminative existence questions (Apache-2.0, https://github.com/junyangwang0410/AMBER). Baselines: GLSim, PAS, Jiang's internal-confidence logit lens, a HALP-style linear probe, and VL-Uncertainty sampling. Report AUROC/PR-AUC and a cost column (forward passes, VRAM).
  * Secondary study (Part B, cheap, ties to Kalai/HalluLens): build a 'NonExistentObjectRefusal' set by rewriting POPE adversarial/popular negatives as open-ended false-premise prompts ('Describe the {object} in the image'), with real-object controls; score with HalluLens' false acceptance rate, Whitehead's Effective Reliability, and Kalai-style penalized scoring with an explicit IDK option. Then test whether the Part A internal signal predicts when the model should have abstained (the representation-action gap, object-level). Position against MM-AQA, HaloQuest, VQAv2-IDK, TUBench, MM-UPD and Rep2Act explicitly in related work.
  * Model and stack for the GTX 1060 (6 GB, Pascal CC 6.1): Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0, https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct) or Qwen2-VL-2B / InternVL 1B (both already supported in the VL-Uncertainty repo). Load with bitsandbytes NF4 (docs confirm NF4/FP4 on CC 6.0+; use CUDA 11.8-12.6 wheels because the 12.8+ builds drop sm60), fp16/fp32 compute (no bf16, no flash-attention on Pascal), attn_implementation='eager' so output_attentions works, and cap image tokens (max_pixels) to ~256-512 so attention maps fit. Validate memory on 10 samples before scaling.
  * Compute and money estimate (assumptions stated): POPE 3x3000 + AMBER discriminative ~14k yes/no questions need one forward pass each (read the yes/no logits, no generation). At an assumed 1-3 s per forward on the 1060 with a 2B NF4 model that is roughly 10-25 GPU-hours, run overnight at zero cost; attention dumps add disk, not time. Generative CHAIR captions are the expensive part (assume ~0.3-0.5 s/token on Pascal): do a 200-300 image subset locally (2-4 h) rather than all 1,004 AMBER images. Cloud only for a 7B replication (LLaVA-1.5-7B or Qwen2.5-VL-7B in fp16): RunPod prices verified 2026-10-12 are RTX 4090 $0.34/h community, $0.74/h secure; L40S $0.79/$1.09; A100 80GB $1.19-1.59/h; H100 PCIe $1.99-2.89/h (https://www.runpod.io/pricing). Lambda: A10 $1.29/h, A100 40GB $1.99/h, H100 SXM $4.29/h (https://lambda.ai/pricing). Budget 20-40 GPU-hours on a 4090 ≈ $7-30, or 20 h on an A100 80GB ≈ $24-32; a safe ceiling for the whole project is ~$50-100. Avoid GPT/Gemini judges; use a local NLI model for semantic clustering and cap any API spend at a few hundred items.
  * Cite as canonical: POPE (https://github.com/RUCAIBox/POPE), CHAIR (Rohrbach et al. 2018, https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.02156, not opened this session), AMBER, OPERA, HALC, Jiang et al. ICLR 2025, Reliable VQA (ECCV 2022), MM-UPD (ACL 2025), HaloQuest (ECCV 2024), Kalai et al. 2025 and HalluLens; cite the 2026 items (HALP, UniProbe, Same Attention, CADMP, MM-AQA, Rep2Act, VSE, VAUQ) to show currency and to pre-empt reviewer 'already done' objections.
  * Avoid as the main contribution: (a) another decoding-method comparison (OPERA/HALC/VCD-style) because it is saturated and generation-heavy; (b) a plain linear probe on hidden states as the headline (HALP, EnsemHalDet, UniProbe); (c) 'VLMs rarely abstain' or 'VLMs are miscalibrated' as findings (MM-AQA, Rep2Act, Xuan et al., Zhao et al.); (d) synthetic benchmark generation that needs GPT-4V/DALL-E (AutoHallusion pipeline) given the API budget.
  * Replicate first, then extend: reproduce Jiang's internal-confidence detector and GLSim on the 2B model and POPE as week 1-2 sanity checks (they are training-free), which also gives the report a solid replication section if the novel signal underperforms.
  * Report framing for a later workshop paper: 'Do text-only hallucination detectors and scoring rules transfer to object hallucination?' with two tables (detection AUROC by signal family and modality block; leaderboard re-ranking under abstention-aware scoring) is a complete, defensible story at ~6-8 weeks part-time.

-- cautions --
  * Explicit search result: no LVLM application of LLM-Check was found. This is a negative result from web search, not a guarantee; the Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination list (https://github.com/NishilBalar/Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination) was only read up to ~200k of 256k characters.
  * Several items are 2026 arXiv preprints read only from their abs pages (UniProbe, CADMP, MM-AQA, Rep2Act, EnsemHalDet, Spectral QK, VSE, VAUQ); numbers quoted are from abstracts and may change on revision. Code availability for HALP, UniProbe, GLSim, PAS, CADMP, Same Attention ('will be available'), Rep2Act and MM-AQA was not confirmed.
  * Venues not confirmed on the primary page: the visual attention sink paper (reported as ICLR 2025) and 'Devils in Middle Layers' (listed only as arXiv). Treat them as arXiv until checked.
  * Licenses: only POPE (MIT), AMBER (Apache-2.0), AutoHallusion (BSD-3), Qwen3-VL-2B (Apache-2.0) and HalluLens (CC-BY-NC, non-commercial) were verified; OPERA, HALC, Reliable VQA, MM-UPD, HaloQuest, TUBench and Jiang et al. licenses were not checked, and the VL-Uncertainty repo shows no license file.
  * GTX 1060 throughput figures (1-3 s per forward, 0.3-0.5 s per generated token) are my estimates, not measurements; Pascal has very slow fp16 and no bf16 or flash-attention, and bitsandbytes NF4 on CC 6.1 is supported per the official docs but may be slower than on newer GPUs. The Qwen3-VL-2B model card recommends bf16 plus flash_attention_2, neither of which the 1060 supports, so an fp16/fp32 eager fallback must be tested early.
  * Cloud prices were verified on 2026-10-12 for RunPod and Lambda only; Vast.ai prices are dynamic and were not readable; all prices exclude tax and change frequently.
  * Kalai et al.'s abs page contains no multimodal mention; the earlier search summary claiming the analysis 'covers text or multimodal inputs' could not be confirmed on the primary page and should not be cited that way.
  * Reference-free Hallucination Detection for LVLMs (https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05767, Aug 2024) already compares uncertainty-, consistency- and supervised UQ-based detection on four LVLMs and finds supervised UQ best; include it in related work so the detector comparison is not presented as new.
  * PhantomBench (https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11105, 2026, non-existent concepts) appeared in search results but was not opened; it appears text-only and is unverified here.

-- confidence_notes -- High confidence on the canonical items (OPERA, HALC, Jiang ICLR 2025, Reliable VQA, MM-UPD, HaloQuest, POPE, AMBER, HalluLens structure, Kalai abstract) since each primary page was opened. Medium confidence on details of the 2025-2026 preprints, which were read from arXiv abs pages only (abstract-level numbers, no full-text check, code often unconfirmed). The two headline negative findings (no LLM-Check-to-LVLM port; no exact image analog of NonExistentRefusal) rest on multiple targeted searches plus a partial read of a large curated list, so they are likely but not certain. The compute and cost estimates combine verified prices and documented hardware constraints with unverified per-sample throughput guesses; treat the dollar range as an order-of-magnitude budget.


######## ANGLE: latest ########
angle: Newest work (Oct 2025 - Oct 2026) on object/visual hallucination in MLLMs (benchmarks, mitigation, detection, analyses, surveys) plus the newest small open-weight VLMs (<= ~4B) with exact Hugging Face ids, licenses, quantized variants and any reported POPE/HallusionBench scores; with trends, saturation map, and must-cite list for a late-2026 reviewer.

[1] Kalai, Nachum, Vempala, Zhang - 'Evaluating large language models for accuracy incentivizes hallucinations' (Nature journal version of OpenAI's 'Why Language Models Hallucinate') (2026; Nature 653(8116):1047-1051; online 22 Apr 2026, print 28 May 2026) verified=True
    url: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10549-w
    what: Journal version of the preprint on the advisor's list: learning-theory argument that rare facts inevitably yield errors and that accuracy-only scoring rewards confident guessing over abstention; proposes 'open rubric' evaluations with explicit error penalties and hallucination-aware variants of existing benchmarks.
    why: A late-2026 reviewer will expect the Nature citation, not only arXiv 2509.04664. Its incentive argument maps directly onto POPE-style yes/no scoring, which gives zero credit to 'cannot tell' - the hook for an abstention-aware object-hallucination metric.
    code/data: none (metadata verified via Crossref API; Nature page sits behind a cookie redirect)

[2] SHROOM-Visions 2026 shared task (SHEEP dataset) + 'Can Humans Dream of Electric Sheep?' human-written span annotations (2026; UncertaiNLP workshop @ EMNLP 2026 (overview paper marked 'under review')) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25662
    what: Model-agnostic, span-level hallucination detection in image-conditioned generation across EN/FR/IT/ZH with a five-class taxonomy; 27 teams, 600+ systems, best ~0.58 character-level correlation (30-40 points over baselines). Companion paper arXiv 2608.01021 (Aug 2026) releases 1,600 human-written plus 18,400 model-generated span-annotated samples from five VLMs. One entry (arXiv 2609.10244) fine-tuned a 4B VLM as a per-token detector.
    why: The newest community benchmark for VLM hallucination detection and a reusable annotated dataset; shows a 4B model can serve as a detector, which fits the GTX 1060 budget.
    code/data: Dataset/task links not on the arXiv abstract page; not opened (mark as to-verify)

[3] FREAK: Fine-grained Hallucination Evaluation Benchmark for Advanced MLLMs (2026; ICLR 2026 (per GitHub README title and ICLR proceedings link found in search; arXiv abstract lists no venue)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19765
    what: 1,799 questions over photorealistic images carrying fine-grained counter-commonsense edits produced by a 'generate-then-edit' pipeline (synthesize a plausible image, then edit in one localized anomaly); six categories (detection, counting, attribute, analysis, position, OCR). SOTA MLLMs reach ~45% vs 86.7% for humans; includes CoT control subsets.
    why: Closest published template for the 'synthetic visual data' half of the project title: synthetic edits give exact ground truth (no annotation noise) and directly stress language priors.
    code/data: https://github.com/Hans-M-Yin/FREAK (link from search results, repo not opened)

[4] CIPHER + OHC-25K: Fighting Hallucinations with Counterfactuals (Diffusion-Guided Perturbations) (2026; CVPR 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10470
    what: Builds OHC-25K, 25,000 diffusion-edited counterfactual images paired with the unmodified ground-truth captions, uses them to locate a low-rank 'hallucination subspace' in LVLM hidden states, and projects hidden states away from it at inference (training-free).
    why: Demonstrates diffusion-edited counterfactuals as both an evaluation and a mitigation resource; a feature-level projection is cheap enough to run on 2B-4B models on a 6 GB GPU.
    code/data: Project page https://hamidreza-dastmalchi.github.io/cipher-cvpr2026/ (no repo link on the CVPR page)

[5] HII-DPO (Hallucination-Inducing Images + Masked-Object-Hallucination benchmark), with POPEv2/Obliviate lineage (2026 (HII-DPO); 2025 (POPEv2); arXiv preprints (cs.CV)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10425
    what: HII-DPO (Feb 2026) synthesizes images with scene-typical objects removed, shows models still mention them, and builds a DPO preference set from these images (up to 38% gain on standard hallucination benchmarks). POPEv2 / Obliviate (arXiv 2508.04567, Aug 2025) masks objects in LVLM training images, shows models hallucinate more on images seen in training, and unlearns via the LM head only (~2% of parameters) across 2B-72B models.
    why: These two define the 'counterfactual masked image' recipe a reviewer will expect in any synthetic-data hallucination project; Obliviate's tiny trainable footprint is the only training-time mitigation plausibly feasible on a 1060 for a 2B model.
    code/data: none listed on either abstract page ('will be released')

[6] RePOPE: Impact of Annotation Errors on the POPE Benchmark (2025; arXiv preprint (cs.CV)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15707
    what: Re-annotates the MSCOCO images used by POPE, finds label errors distributed unevenly across the random/popular/adversarial subsets, and shows notable model-ranking shifts after correction.
    why: Any POPE-style evaluation on real images in this project should use RePOPE labels; it is also the cleanest motivation for synthetic images with known ground truth.
    code/data: https://github.com/YanNeu/RePOPE

[7] DO-Bench (attributable object-hallucination diagnosis) and the 2026 benchmark wave (FINER, MIOH, UniHall, ReactBench, VOPE) (2026; DO-Bench: arXiv (Apr 2026); FINER and MIOH: CVPR 2026; VOPE: ACM MM 2026 dataset track; UniHall, ReactBench: arXiv) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22822
    what: DO-Bench isolates mechanisms with two axes - strengthen textual priors while holding visual evidence fixed, and enlarge visual evidence from full scene to object crop - yielding PriorRobust and PerceptionAbility metrics. Same window: FINER (arXiv 2603.17662; fine-grained negative queries, FINER-Tuning DPO up to +24.2% on InternVL3.5-14B), MIOH multi-image object hallucination (arXiv 2608.30653; 29 models incl. GPT-5), UniHall/SAMF evolutionary fuzzing (arXiv 2608.07525; finds a helpfulness-hallucination trade-off), ReactBench cause-driven adversarial tasks (arXiv 2605.29579), VOPE voluntary-imagination grounding (arXiv 2511.13420).
    why: Shows what 'new benchmark' means in 2026: mechanism-attributable, adversarial, multi-image. A 6-8 week project should borrow one design (DO-Bench's prior-vs-evidence axes fit synthetic edits perfectly) rather than propose a POPE clone.
    code/data: UniHall: https://github.com/LanceZPF/EvalHall; FINER: explainableml.github.io/finer-project; ReactBench: reactbench.github.io; DO-Bench/MIOH/VOPE: none on abstract pages

[8] HumbleBench (epistemic humility, 'None of the above') and the 2026 abstention/refusal line (2025 (v2 May 2026); arXiv (HumbleBench); VLM-DeflectionBench: ACL 2026; SAVOR: ICONIP 2026; others arXiv) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09658
    what: HumbleBench: 22,831 MCQs over object/relation/attribute hallucinations built from panoptic scene graphs, each with a 'None of the above' option to test false-option rejection. Related 2026 work: VLM-DeflectionBench (ACL 2026; 2,775 samples; models 'usually fail to deflect' under misleading evidence), Visual-Idk knowledge-boundary tuning (arXiv 2604.26419; Truthful Rate 57.9 to 67.3), SAVOR confidence-calibrated GRPO (arXiv 2609.16601), 'To See or To Please' (arXiv 2603.18373; 72.9% visual sycophancy across 9 VLMs, zero robust refusal), SycoPhantasy (arXiv 2604.24346; 450M-8B open VLMs on 173,810 AI-generated portraits, smaller = more sycophantic).
    why: This is the multimodal counterpart of HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal task and of Kalai's abstention argument. No paper yet builds an abstention-aware object-existence test for <=4B models, which is an open and cheap contribution.
    code/data: https://github.com/maifoundations/HumbleBench; DeflectionBench resources 'upon publication'; others none

[9] Evaluation pitfalls: 'Does Playing it Safe Count as Faithfulness?' and GHOST-Q (quantization vs grounding) (2026; arXiv (Sept 2026); GHOST-Q submitted to IEEE ICASSP 2027) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01888
    what: Fazli et al. re-run six mitigation methods on three LVLMs and four benchmarks: hallucination reductions are coupled with lower informativeness and do not transfer to general capability; they argue for a faithfulness-informativeness-capability trade-off. GHOST-Q (arXiv 2609.29999) pairs FP16 vs INT8/NF4 predictions item-by-item on three 8B VLM families: MMStar stays within +-2 points yet 10 of 36 paired effects are significant, nine on hallucination-sensitive conditions; AMBER audit reveals generation-budget censoring. A related preprint questions contrastive decoding itself (arXiv 2607.25196, not opened).
    why: Defines the protocol a 2026 reviewer expects (report coverage/informativeness, not CHAIR alone) and turns 4-bit quantization - unavoidable on a 6 GB card - into a legitimate experimental variable; GHOST-Q only covered 8B models, leaving <=4B at Q4 open.
    code/data: none found

[10] Training-free mitigation wave 2026 - anchor: AIR (Attention Imbalance Rectification) (2026; CVPR 2026 Findings (AIR); ResDec CVPR 2026; AFTER ICLR 2026; SAE ACM MM 2026; others arXiv) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24058
    what: AIR reallocates modality-wise and token-wise attention at decoding time; up to 35.1% hallucination reduction on CHAIR/POPE/MM-Vet across four LVLMs with up to 15.9% general-capability gain. Same wave: Residual Decoding (arXiv 2602.01047), AFTER activation editing (arXiv 2601.01957; -16.3% on AMBER), CAST caption-guided attention steering (arXiv 2605.04641; -6.03% avg over 5 models/5 benchmarks), FADE FFN attenuation (arXiv 2606.29431), Semantic-Spatial Agreement Verification (arXiv 2609.17269; LLaVA-1.5-7B hallucination 49.4% to 32.8%), ResOT optimal-transport 'repair' (arXiv 2610.11826, 8 Oct 2026), Segmentation-based Attention Entropy (arXiv 2603.16558).
    why: About 40 object-hallucination papers appeared on arXiv in the last 12 months and nearly all evaluate on LLaVA-1.5-7B/InstructBLIP; replicating the two or three with code on 2026 small natively-multimodal models is a feasible, publishable 'does it still work?' study.
    code/data: AIR: https://github.com/Ice-wave/AIR; most others 'code will be released' (ResDec, CAST, FADE, SSAV, ResOT, SAE: none on abstract pages)

[11] Detection from internal states (LLM-Check analogs) - anchor: Instruction Lens Score (InsLen) (2026; ICML 2026 (InsLen); HALP EACL 2026; VisER and SAFE EMNLP 2026; others arXiv) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12258
    what: InsLen reads instruction-token embeddings and combines a Calibrated Local Score with a Context Consistency Score; training-free, no auxiliary model. HALP (arXiv 2603.05465) probes pre-generation representations of eight VLMs, up to 0.93 AUROC. VisER (arXiv 2608.30480) scores Visual Evidence vs Visual Reliance per object. SAFE (arXiv 2609.16646) contrasts grounded vs vision-ablated paths and finds visual dependency decays along generation. Mechanistic companions: Dual-Pathway Circuits (arXiv 2605.13156; suppressing hallucination components cuts object hallucination up to 76%) and Contrastive Neuron Steering via SAEs (arXiv 2602.00621).
    why: Direct bridge from LLM-Check's hidden-state/attention/logit detection to VLMs; probes need only forward passes and have not been reported on Qwen3.5, Gemma 4 or LFM2.5 small models.
    code/data: InsLen: https://github.com/Fraserlairh/Instruction-Lens-Score; SAFE: https://github.com/zhaozhipeng1997/SAFE_public; DualPath: https://github.com/jiaxin26/DualPath-VLM; HALP/VisER: none

[12] Reasoning-hallucination trade-off: 'More Thinking, Less Seeing?' (RH-Bench) and 'When Thinking Hurts' (2025 / 2026; NeurIPS 2025 (inferred from a proceedings.neurips.cc link in search results; arXiv abstract lists no venue); 'When Thinking Hurts' oral at EIML@ICML 2026) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21523
    what: Introduces RH-AUC and RH-Bench: longer reasoning chains reduce attention to the image and amplify perception hallucination; larger models balance better and data mix matters more than volume. 'When Thinking Hurts' (arXiv 2607.08059) finds thinking-chain entropy beats answer entropy as an uncertainty signal and shows an abstention gate raising accuracy from 71% to 93.8% at reduced coverage.
    why: 2026 small models (Qwen3.5, Qwen3-VL-Thinking, Gemma 4) ship with thinking on by default; Qwen3.5-2B's own card shows HallusionBench 58.0 (thinking) vs 51.3 (non-thinking) - a free experimental factor nobody has studied for object hallucination at <=4B.
    code/data: none on abstract pages

[13] A Survey of Multimodal Hallucination Evaluation and Detection (v2, Jan 2026) plus maintained awesome lists (2026 (v2); 2025 (v1); arXiv (40 pages; CAS/UCAS/Huawei)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19024
    what: Taxonomy by faithfulness vs factuality, covering image-to-text and text-to-image benchmarks and detection methods. Supplement with github.com/NishilBalar/Awesome-LVLM-Hallucination (marked up to date 31 Mar 2026; lists PIH, CFHR, CSR-Bench, HalDec-Bench, FINER and 250+ mitigation entries with code links) - the showlab list showed no 2026 entries.
    why: Most recent survey to cite as the multimodal counterpart of Huang et al. 2023; no dedicated 2026 object-hallucination survey was found, so related work must be assembled from this plus the arXiv feed.
    code/data: n/a; awesome list opened and dated

Vendor HallusionBench: 2B 58.0/51.3 (thinking/non-thinking); 4B 65.0 (table also lists Qwen3.5-9B 69.3, Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B 66.0, Gemini-2.5-Flash-Lite 64.5); 4B MMBench 89.4, MMMU 77.6; no POPE on card.
