######## ANGLE: FILL: Where the hallucination originates: encoder probes, CLIP-blind pairs (MMVP), self-grounding checks, and the missing bridge to Xu et al. ########
angle: Where object hallucination originates and how to prove it cheaply: encoder-level probes (CLIP-blind pairs, OHD-Caps, F-CLIPScore, Pfram), blind text-only baselines, internal-state/attention detectors that mirror LLM-Check, synthetic-image embedding shift, self-grounding verification with sub-4B grounding models, and the formal bridge from Xu et al. and Kalai et al. to conditional (caption/VQA) generation.

[1] Eyes Wide Shut? Exploring the Visual Shortcomings of Multimodal LLMs (MMVP, CLIP-blind pairs) (2024; CVPR 2024 (arXiv 2401.06209, v2 Apr 2024)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06209
    what: Mines CLIP-blind pairs from ImageNet/LAION-Aesthetics: CLIP ViT-L/14 cosine > 0.95 but DINOv2 ViT-L/14 cosine < 0.6, giving 150 pairs / 300 questions over 9 visual patterns. Humans 95.7%, GPT-4V ~38.7%, LLaVA-1.5 and InstructBLIP below the 25% chance level; MMVP-VLM shows 7 of 9 patterns unsolved by any CLIP model and CLIP failures correlate with open MLLM failures (Pearson > 0.7). Interleaved Mixture-of-Features (CLIP+DINOv2 tokens) adds +10.7% on MMVP.
    why: Settles the encoder-vs-LLM question at the data level and gives a reusable mining recipe: the student can build object-level CLIP-blind pairs on COCO (same CLIP similarity, different COCO object annotations) at zero annotation cost. Cheapest 6 GB version: MMVP is 300 images / 3 MB; CLIP-L and DINOv2-L run in fp32 under 2 GB each; any 2-3B VLM answers the 300 questions in minutes.
    code/data: https://github.com/tsb0601/MMVP (MIT); dataset https://huggingface.co/datasets/MMVP/MMVP (MIT, 300 images, 3.16 MB); MMVP-VLM https://huggingface.co/datasets/MMVP/MMVP_VLM

[2] Investigating and Mitigating Object Hallucinations in Pretrained Vision-Language (CLIP) Models (OHD-Caps) (2024; EMNLP 2024) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03176
    what: Shows CLIP hallucinates objects in isolation: on OHD-Caps (500 images each from COCO/Flickr30k/NoCaps with 27 negatives per image built by SEEM segmentation plus GPT-4 object insertion/removal in random/popular/adversarial flavours), CLIP ViT-L/14 ranks the correct caption first only 19.0% of the time; 17+ encoders tested incl. SigLIP, EVA-CLIP, MetaCLIP, DFN. A counterfactual contrastive fine-tune fixes CLIP and raises LLaVA-1.5 POPE F1 and lowers CHAIR when the encoder is swapped in.
    why: Direct, benchmarked evidence that part of object hallucination is born in the encoder, using POPE's own random/popular/adversarial taxonomy, so encoder and full-VLM errors can be scored on matched items. Cheapest 6 GB version: scoring 1,500 test images x 28 captions with CLIP/SigLIP in fp32 is minutes; only the fine-tune belongs in the cloud.
    code/data: https://github.com/Yufang-Liu/clip_hallucination (benchmark + fine-tuned checkpoints on HF; no license stated in README)

[3] Do Vision Encoders Truly Explain Object Hallucination? Mitigating Object Hallucination via Simple Fine-Grained CLIPScore (F-CLIPScore) (2025 (TMLR; arXiv v1 Feb 2025, updated Jan 2026); Transactions on Machine Learning Research) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20034
    what: Argues encoder capacity is not the limit: a noun-level CLIPScore (text embeddings per noun) improves OHD-Caps accuracy by 39.6% over plain CLIPScore with no training, and filtering alignment-pretraining data with it raises POPE by 4.9%.
    why: This is the cheap encoder-level probe the gap asks for and the counter-position to OHD-Caps; together they frame 'encoder-blind' versus 'encoder-readout-blind', which the report can test directly. Cheapest 6 GB version: a few CLIP text/image forward passes per caption; the arXiv-listed code URL (github.com/abzb1/f-clip) returned 404 on 2026-10-12, so reimplement from the paper.
    code/data: arXiv lists https://github.com/abzb1/f-clip (404 when checked); no license verifiable

[4] Hidden in plain sight: VLMs overlook their visual representations (2025; arXiv (June 2025)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08008
    what: Compares Prismatic VLMs (DINOv2-L/14, ViT-IN1k-L/16, CLIP-L/14, SigLIP-L/14 + Vicuna-7B) plus Qwen-VL, Phi-3-V and InternVL against a direct readout of their own encoders (probes, cosine matching) on CV-Bench depth, BLINK correspondence, MOCHI and art style: the readout is near ceiling while the VLM is near chance (DINOv2 semantic correspondence 53.6% -> 22.5%; low-level matching -45.5 pts; depth -21.7 pts). A blank-image 'blind_vlm' control shows VLM answer distributions mirror blind answers, and fine-tuning the LLM (equal parameter budget) helps most, so the LLM is the bottleneck.
    why: Supplies the exact three-way protocol (probe on frozen encoder vs sighted VLM vs blind VLM) that the student should port from depth/correspondence to object presence; strongest 2025 evidence for the language-side origin. Cheapest 6 GB version: cache encoder features once, fit logistic probes on CPU; blind runs are cheaper than sighted ones.
    code/data: https://github.com/stephanie-fu/hidden-plain-sight (probes and blind_vlm condition; no license file noted); project page https://hidden-plain-sight.github.io/

[5] Understanding Multimodal Hallucination with Parameter-Free Representation Alignment (Pfram) (2024; arXiv (Sep 2024)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01151
    what: A training-free metric that measures how well an image-representation system (vision encoder output or an LLM layer) aligns with human ground-truth object annotations; alignment correlates strongly and consistently with POPE object hallucination across InstructBLIP-7B/13B, LLaVA, Muffin and RLHF-V on OpenImages V7 and AMBER, pointing at the visual-encoding stage.
    why: Lets the student attribute hallucination to representation stages without training any probe and complements OHD-Caps/F-CLIPScore/Hidden-in-plain-sight. Cheapest 6 GB version: runs on cached features plus COCO annotations.
    code/data: https://github.com/yellow-binary-tree/Pfram (no license stated)

[6] Blind baselines: Debiasing MLLMs via Penalization of Language Priors (Post-Hoc Debias / VDD) and M3ID (Multi-Modal Mutual-Information Decoding) (2024 (M3ID CVPR 2024; VDD arXiv Mar 2024, ACM MM 2025 per repo); CVPR 2024 (M3ID, arXiv 2403.14003) / ACM Multimedia 2025 (VDD)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05262
    what: VDD shows MLLMs answer confidently with absent or incongruent images and contrasts logits conditioned on the real image versus a meaningless one; its repo defines the blind POPE baselines (pure text, </unk>-replaced vision tokens, Gaussian noise, all-zeros/ones images). M3ID shows reliance on the image decays as generation proceeds and amplifies pointwise mutual information with the image over the unconditioned prior: LLaVA-13B gets -25% hallucinated caption objects and +21% POPE (M3ID+DPO: -28% / +24%).
    why: The text-only blind POPE baseline is the cheapest measurement of how much of a sub-4B model's score is language prior; report blind vs sighted vs encoder-probe accuracy on identical items. Cheapest 6 GB version: blind runs need no image tokens, so they are faster than the sighted runs.
    code/data: https://github.com/yfzhang114/LLaVA-Align (Apache-2.0; LLaVA-1.5-7B/13B scripts); M3ID: no code listed on arXiv

[7] AIGCs Confuse AI Too: Investigating and Explaining Synthetic Image-induced Hallucinations in LVLMs (2024; arXiv (Mar 2024, v2 Sep 2024)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08542
    what: Finds AI-generated images induce more object hallucinations with a more uniform spatial distribution than natural images, even when the synthetic images show no unrealistic or extra features, and traces the bias to token deviations after the Q-Former / linear projector.
    why: The direct precedent for the title's synthetic-vs-real axis and for asking whether generated or inpainted images shift embeddings; the student can replicate the deviation analysis on CLIP/SigLIP pooled features and projector outputs of a 2-3B model. Cheapest 6 GB version: embedding-shift comparison is forward passes only.
    code/data: none found (no code URL on arXiv page)

[8] Synthetic-image hallucination benchmarks: ODE (open-set dynamic), AutoHallusion (inpainting-based), VHTest (CLIP-space seeded) (2024-2025; ODE arXiv 2409.09318 (v4 Jul 2025); AutoHallusion EMNLP 2024 (arXiv 2406.10900); VHTest ACL Findings 2024 (arXiv 2402.14683)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09318
    what: ODE builds a concept graph from AMBER's 337 objects, renders combinations with FLUX.1-dev / Stable Diffusion 1.5, discards images where an open-vocabulary detector scores < 0.65, and validates realism by comparing CLIP features of synthetic vs natural images in 2-D; it evaluates 8 MLLMs incl. Qwen2-VL and InternVL-2.5. AutoHallusion inserts abnormal/paired objects or removes correlated objects (OWL-ViT, rembg) and reaches 97.7% / 98.7% hallucination induction on GPT-4V, Gemini Pro Vision, Claude 3 and LLaVA-1.5, releasing 3,129 cases. VHTest seeds 1,200 visual-hallucination instances from COCO in CLIP embedding space and renders them with DALL-E 3.
    why: Ready-made synthetic items and the exact CLIP-space comparison the gap mentions, plus a methodological warning: detector-filtered generation bakes an external detector's notion of object presence into the benchmark, which the report should state and avoid. Cheapest 6 GB version: use the released images; SD1.5 inpainting at 512 px fits 6 GB (slow on Pascal), FLUX does not.
    code/data: https://github.com/Iridescent-y/ODE (no license stated); https://github.com/wuxiyang1996/AutoHallusion (BSD-3-Clause); https://github.com/wenhuang2000/VHTest (Apache-2.0)

[9] Internal-state probes: Interpreting and Editing Vision-Language Representations to Mitigate Hallucinations (logit-lens internal confidence) + Towards Interpreting Visual Information Processing in VLMs (2025; ICLR 2025 (both; second paper arXiv 2410.07149)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02762
    what: Jiang et al. project image-token representations through the LLM unembedding (logit lens) and define internal confidence as the max object-token probability over image tokens and layers; hallucinated objects score lower (detection +47.17% on InstructBLIP, +22.45% on LLaVA-1.5), and orthogonalising image features against the hallucinated object's text embedding cuts COCO2014 hallucinations by up to 25.7% with zero-shot segmentation as a by-product. Neo et al. show object information is localised to specific image-token positions (ablating them drops identification by >70%) and is read out at the last token position.
    why: The multimodal analogue of LLM-Check's hidden-state detector on the advisor's list: a free per-object score from the same forward pass, which the student can replicate on a 2-3B model with HF hooks. Cheapest 6 GB version: one forward pass with output_hidden_states on Qwen2-VL-2B / Qwen3-VL-2B in 4-bit.
    code/data: https://github.com/nickjiang2378/vl-interp (CC0-1.0; LLaVA-1.5/NeXT, InstructBLIP-7B, Cambrian); Neo et al.: no code URL on abs page

[10] Attention-pattern detectors: DHCP (ACM MM 2025), Devils in the Middle Layers (CVPR 2025), EAZY (ICCV 2025) (2024-2025; ACM Multimedia 2025 (DHCP); CVPR 2025 (arXiv 2411.16724); ICCV 2025 (arXiv 2503.07772)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18659
    what: DHCP averages generated-token-to-image-token attention over layers and heads and trains a 2-layer MLP (128 hidden): ~87% F1 on POPE-COCO for Qwen2.5-VL-7B, InternVL-2.5-4B, LLaVA-1.5-7B and InstructBLIP-7B, degrading to ~42% precision on out-of-distribution POPE-GQA and 53% F1 on AMBER. Devils-in-middle-layers finds real object tokens receive more visual attention than hallucinated ones in a middle-layer 'visual information enrichment' stage and proposes heads-guided attention intervention (CHAIR on LLaVA-1.5-7B). EAZY finds about 1.5% of high-attention image tokens drive object hallucination; zeroing them improves detection by 15%.
    why: These are the vision-side counterparts of LLM-Check's attention-kernel-map features, letting the report present one detector family across text-only and multimodal settings; DHCP is the only one already run on a sub-5B model (InternVL-2.5-4B), so porting the probe to 2-3B models is new. Cheapest 6 GB version: attention extraction on a 2B model in 4-bit over one 3k-question POPE split (DHCP's 408k-sample extraction is unnecessary).
    code/data: https://github.com/btzyd/DHCP (Qwen2.5-VL-7B scripts; no license stated); https://github.com/ZhangqiJiang07/middle_layers_indicating_hallucinations (no license stated); EAZY: no code URL on abs page

[11] Self-verification precedents (LogicCheckGPT, Woodpecker, HALC, VisualFactChecker, GRIT) and sub-4B models that can ground their own claims (2024-2025; ACL 2024 (LogicCheckGPT); SCIS 2024 (Woodpecker, arXiv 2310.16045); ICML 2024 (HALC, arXiv 2403.00425); CVPR 2024 (VFC, arXiv 2404.19752); NeurIPS 2025 (GRIT, arXiv 2505.15879)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11622
    what: LogicCheckGPT detects hallucinated objects by checking whether the model's own object-to-attribute and attribute-to-object answers close a logical loop (mPLUG-Owl, LLaVA-1.5, MiniGPT-4, Qwen-VL-Chat on POPE/MME). Woodpecker, HALC and VisualFactChecker verify named objects with an external Grounding DINO / OWLv2 detector or VQA model. GRIT trains Qwen2.5-VL-3B and InternVL-3-2B by RL to emit bounding boxes inside their reasoning from as few as 20 examples, but evaluates VSR/TallyQA/GQA/MathVista/MME/OVDEval, not hallucination. Among all sources opened, no paper uses a model's own detect/point output to verify the objects it just named, on any <4B model.
    why: Pins down the gap: self-consistency checks and external-detector verification exist, and 2-3B models can ground natively, but self-grounding consistency as a hallucination detector is untested. Models that fit 6 GB: Qwen2.5-VL-3B (absolute-pixel boxes/points in JSON; Qwen Research License = non-commercial; GGUF Q4_K_M 1.93 GB + mmproj Q8_0 845 MB), Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct (Apache-2.0; boxes and points), Moondream2 (2B, Apache-2.0, model.detect(image, name) / model.point(...); GGUF F16 2.84 GB), Florence-2 base/large (0.23B/0.77B, MIT; <CAPTION>, <OD>, <CAPTION_TO_PHRASE_GROUNDING>), PaliGemma 2 3B mix-448 (Gemma licence; 'detect cat ; dog'). llama.cpp's mtmd lists Qwen2-VL, Qwen2.5-VL, Moondream2, SmolVLM, InternVL and Gemma 3 as supported.
    code/data: https://github.com/Hyperwjf/LogicCheckGPT (MIT); https://github.com/BillChan226/HALC (BSD-3; detectors 'dino'/'owlv2'); https://github.com/BradyFU/Woodpecker (GroundingDINO + BLIP-2; license not stated); https://github.com/eric-ai-lab/GRIT; models: https://huggingface.co/ggml-org/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct-GGUF, https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct, https://huggingface.co/vikhyatk/moondream2, https://huggingface.co/microsoft/Florence-2-large, https://huggingface.co/google/paligemma2-3b-mix-448, https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/docs/multimodal.md

[12] Theory bridge: Xu et al. 'Hallucination is Inevitable', Kalai & Vempala 'Calibrated LMs Must Hallucinate', Kalai et al. 'Why Language Models Hallucinate' (Theorem 1 with prompts), Banerjee et al.; abstention analogues UPD and Reliable VQA (2024-2025; arXiv 2401.11817 (v2 Feb 2025); STOC 2024 (arXiv 2311.14648); arXiv 2509.04664 (Sep 2025); arXiv 2409.05746; ACL 2025 (UPD, arXiv 2403.20331); ECCV 2022 (arXiv 2204.13631)) verified=True
    url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11817
    what: Xu et al. model an LLM as a total computable function from strings to strings and prove (Thms 1-3) that for every computable LLM, or computably enumerable set of them, there is a computable ground-truth f on which it hallucinates on infinitely many inputs; the paper never mentions images, vision or conditional generation, but an image is a finite string, so the theorems apply verbatim to MLLMs, with the caveat that f is adversarially constructed (inevitability for general problem solving, not for the fixed object-presence task). Kalai & Vempala's lower bound is the Good-Turing 'monofact' rate for arbitrary facts, which image objects are not. Kalai et al. 2025 give the usable bridge: for any prompt-conditional generator, err >= 2*err_iiv - max_c|V_c|/min_c|E_c| - delta, i.e. generative error is at least twice the Is-It-Valid misclassification rate minus a valid/error set-size ratio and a calibration gap; they also propose explicit confidence targets ('answer only if > t confident, mistakes cost t/(1-t)'). No 2024-2026 paper applying either result to captioning or VQA was found among the sources opened; Banerjee et al. (computability / Goedel, 'structural hallucination') is the only extension and is text-only. UPD (AAD/IASD/IVQD: abstain on unsolvable visual MCQ) and Reliable VQA (effective reliability Phi_c, coverage vs risk) are the multimodal analogues of HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal task and of Kalai et al.'s scoring fix.
    why: Lets the report cite a real formal bridge instead of asserting one: POPE is literally the IIV classification problem for object validity, and open-ended captioning is the generative problem whose error Theorem 1 bounds below by 2*err_iiv; note that for binary POPE questions |V_c| = |E_c| = 1, so the ratio term is 1 and the bound is vacuous, which is why the theory must be tested on CHAIR-style captioning, not on yes/no polling. Cheapest 6 GB version: zero extra compute, just the POPE and CHAIR runs on the same models.
    code/data: Xu et al., Kalai & Vempala, Kalai et al., Banerjee et al.: none; UPD https://github.com/AtsuMiyai/UPD; Reliable VQA https://github.com/facebookresearch/reliable_vqa

-- gaps_and_opportunities --
  * Self-grounding consistency as a hallucination detector is untested: no opened paper asks a model to detect/point the objects it just named and uses the empty or low-IoU result as a hallucination signal, and none does it on a <4B model. Qwen2.5-VL-3B, Qwen3-VL-2B, Moondream2 and Florence-2 all expose grounding natively and fit 6 GB; evaluate detection AUROC against DHCP-style attention probes, logit-lens internal confidence and VL-Uncertainty on the same POPE/CHAIR items.
  * Encoder-blind vs LLM-prior decomposition per item: combine a frozen CLIP/SigLIP linear probe (item correct?), the sighted VLM answer and the blind VLM answer on identical POPE items to label each hallucination as encoder-blind (probe wrong), readout-blind (probe right, VLM wrong, blind VLM wrong = prior) or integration failure (probe right, blind right, sighted wrong). This operationalises a prior-vs-evidence axis at zero annotation cost; no opened paper reports this three-way confusion matrix for small models.
  * Synthetic vs real as a controlled experiment: inpaint-remove one annotated COCO object (SD1.5 inpainting or any released synthetic set) and measure (a) CLIP/SigLIP embedding shift and the probe's drop for the removed class, versus (b) whether the VLM still says 'yes'. This directly tests the AIGCs-Confuse-AI-Too claim (token deviations after projection) and the ODE CLIP-space check with paired real/synthetic images, which none of them do at the object level.
  * Object-level CLIP-blind pairs: apply the MMVP recipe (CLIP cosine > 0.95, DINOv2 cosine < 0.6) to COCO val and additionally require differing object annotations; this yields a free, real-image 'encoder-blind object' set complementary to OHD-Caps, which uses caption negatives rather than image pairs.
  * Theory-to-measurement test: Kalai et al.'s Theorem 1 predicts caption (CHAIR) hallucination >= 2x the model's own IIV error minus small terms; estimate err_iiv from POPE (and from the encoder probe) and check whether the ordering of 3-4 small models and 2-3 decoding methods (greedy, VCD, M3ID) obeys the bound. Nobody has published this check for MLLMs.
  * Abstention in object polling: POPE has no 'I don't know' option, exactly the evaluation flaw Kalai et al. and HalluLens' NonExistentRefusal target. Add an IDK option with a confidence-target instruction and score with Reliable VQA's effective reliability or UPD-style accuracy; report how much of a small model's POPE accuracy is confident guessing.
  * Small-model coverage: DHCP's smallest model is InternVL-2.5-4B, vl-interp and LogicCheckGPT are 7B-class, Hidden-in-plain-sight uses 7B LLMs; replicating any of these detectors on 2-3B models is a contribution on its own and is the only scale the GTX 1060 can run.

-- recommended_for_this_project --
  * Core cite set for the multimodal bridge: POPE (EMNLP 2023, https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10355, MIT code), CHAIR (EMNLP 2018, https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.02156), AMBER (LLM-free, https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.07397), HallusionBench (CVPR 2024, https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.14566), VCD (https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.16922), MMVP, OHD-Caps, F-CLIPScore, Hidden-in-plain-sight, Pfram, VDD/M3ID, vl-interp, DHCP, LogicCheckGPT, Kalai et al. 2025 Theorem 1 with prompts, Xu et al. Thm 2.
  * Replicate first (one week, all on the 1060): MMVP 300 questions and OHD-Caps test split with CLIP ViT-L/14 and SigLIP; blind vs sighted POPE (VDD protocol) on Qwen3-VL-2B, Moondream2 and Florence-2/SmolVLM; a frozen-encoder logistic probe for the 80 COCO classes on cached features. These give the three-way encoder/prior/integration decomposition with no training beyond logistic regression.
  * Make the self-grounding verifier the headline contribution: caption with the model, extract nouns, call its own detect/point for each, and treat 'no box' or box with IoU < 0.5 against COCO masks as a hallucination flag; compare AUROC to a DHCP-style attention MLP and to vl-interp internal confidence on the same captions. Use Qwen3-VL-2B (Apache-2.0) as the primary model so a conference follow-up is unencumbered; Qwen2.5-VL-3B is non-commercial (Qwen Research License).
  * Use Kalai et al. Theorem 1 as the formal bridge and Xu et al. only as the general-impossibility backdrop; state explicitly that the bound is informative for open-ended captioning (CHAIR), not for yes/no POPE, and run the cross-model bound check as a cheap empirical section.
  * Synthetic data: reuse AutoHallusion (3,129 cases, BSD-3), VHTest (1,200, Apache-2.0) and ODE's pipeline rather than generating at scale; if generating, use SD1.5 inpainting at 512 px for a few hundred object-removal pairs and report CLIP/SigLIP embedding shift plus probe drop. Do not use FLUX.1-dev on the 1060.
  * Runtime on the GTX 1060: run VLMs through llama.cpp GGUF (Qwen2.5-VL-3B Q4_K_M 1.93 GB + mmproj 845 MB; Moondream2 F16 2.84 GB) or HF in fp32 for the sub-1B encoders/Florence-2; bitsandbytes docs confirm NF4/FP4 and 8-bit optimizers on compute capability 6.0+ but LLM.int8() needs 7.5+, and its Windows/Linux wheels include sm60 only for CUDA 11.8-12.6, so pin CUDA <= 12.6.
  * Money estimate (official pages, RunPod page dated 2026-09-27; Lambda undated): RunPod RTX 3090 $0.50/h, A40 48 GB $0.59/h, L4 $0.59/h, RTX 4090 $0.89/h, A100 80 GB $1.79/h, H100 PCIe $2.89/h; Lambda A6000 $1.09/h, A10 $1.29/h, A100 40 GB $1.99/h, H100 PCIe $3.29/h. My workload estimate (not measured): per small model, POPE 9k yes/no + 500 CHAIR captions + MMVP 300 + ~2.5k self-grounding calls is roughly 1-3 GPU-hours on a 3090-class card, so 4 models cost about $5-10; an OHD-Caps-style CLIP fine-tune or a LoRA on a 2B VLM adds 2-6 GPU-hours ($2-10). Total cloud spend should stay under $50; the 1060 can do all inference overnight at an estimated 5-10x slower pace, so cloud is justified only for fine-tuning. CHAIR/AMBER/POPE are LLM-free, so proprietary API spend is optional (a GPT/Claude baseline on MMVP's 300 questions is a few dollars).
  * Avoid: training new benchmarks with detector-filtered generation without stating the circularity; bitsandbytes LLM.int8() on Pascal; claiming Xu et al. proves object hallucination specifically is inevitable; quoting the delta term of Kalai et al. Theorem 1 without reading Section 3.2 directly.

-- cautions --
  * The per-turn WebSearch budget was already exhausted when this sweep started, so discovery relied on primary pages I could name (arXiv, GitHub, Hugging Face, pricing pages) plus one curated list (github.com/showlab/Awesome-MLLM-Hallucination); newer 2026 work on the same questions may exist. A follow-up search is recommended for: 2025-2026 theory papers extending Xu et al. or Kalai et al. to multimodal/conditional generation, and any 'self-grounding' detectors published after mid-2025.
  * SalArt-VQA, DO-Bench and HALP (named in the task's gap statement) could not be located or verified without search; treat them as unverified and do not cite them until a primary source is found.
  * F-CLIPScore's arXiv-listed code URL (github.com/abzb1/f-clip) returned HTTP 404 on 2026-10-12; licenses are not stated in the READMEs of OHD-Caps, Pfram, hidden-plain-sight, DHCP, ODE, PhD and Woodpecker, so confirm reuse terms before redistributing their data.
  * Hardware claims not verified from a primary source here: GTX 1060 (Pascal, GP106) has no tensor cores and very low FP16 throughput, and NVIDIA deprecated Pascal in CUDA 12.8 with removal in CUDA 13.x; verify against NVIDIA release notes before choosing PyTorch/CUDA builds. All throughput and GPU-hour figures above are my estimates, not measurements.
  * Kalai et al. Theorem 1 was read through an automated page summary; the set-size ratio term (max_c|V_c| / min_c|E_c|) and the calibration term delta should be checked against Section 3.2 of arXiv 2509.04664 before quoting. The remark that the bound is vacuous for yes/no polling is my inference from that form.
  * Xu et al.'s automated page summary first claimed the result applies 'regardless of input modality'; the full text contains no mention of images or multimodality, so the extension to MLLMs is an argument the report must make itself (image = finite string, MLLM = computable function), not a citation.
  * RunPod prices are from a page dated 2026-09-27 and Lambda's page is undated; cloud prices change frequently. Colab and Vast.ai pricing could not be read (sign-in or dynamic pages).
  * Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct is under the Qwen Research License (non-commercial only); Qwen3-VL-2B/4B are Apache-2.0; PaliGemma 2 uses the Gemma licence. The Moondream docs site now centres on Moondream 3 Preview (9B MoE, 2B active); the 6 GB-friendly model is the moondream2 HF revision 2025-06-21.
  * MMVP-VLM and OHD-Caps measure encoder failures on different objects (visual patterns vs caption negatives); neither is an object-presence probe on COCO, which is why a self-built probe is recommended rather than a reported number.

-- confidence_notes -- All 12 items were verified by opening a primary source (arXiv abs/HTML, GitHub README, Hugging Face card or official pricing page) on 2026-10-12; headline numbers are quoted from those pages. Discovery coverage is the main uncertainty because no web search could run; verification quality is high, completeness is medium. Cost and runtime figures are estimates derived from verified prices and file sizes, not benchmarks.
